CLIN-003230  # DOCUMENT 21 — Monaghan Clinical Complaint Response — 2025-03-25

CLIN-003231  # Department of Neurology — Consultant Neurologist Response

CLIN-003232  **Source:** Four-page PDF supplied directly by the patient.
CLIN-003233  **Document type:** Consultant neurologist response to Quality and Patient Safety complaint.
CLIN-003234  **Date:** 25/03/2025
CLIN-003235  **Author:** Dr Thomas Monaghan, Consultant Neurologist
CLIN-003236  **Recipient:** Quality and Patient Safety Department
CLIN-003237  **Transcription status:** Substantive transcription from supplied PDF. Patient identifiers and non-essential private details have been generalised.

CLIN-003238  ## Response

CLIN-003239  > Thank you for sending me on the complaint, sent to the Quality and Safety Department, from [Patient]. I am truly very, very sorry that this has been [Patient]'s experience. I have known [Patient] for a long time and I did not understand that this was how he felt. I have realised the pain and discomfort that he has gone through with this, as documented including in his most recent letters — but I did not understand that this was how he felt. Therefore I am truly very sorry indeed.

CLIN-003240  > Whatever the underlying cause for the peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies that [Patient] has, it is our belief and understanding that [Patient] has idiopathic Parkinson's disease.

CLIN-003241  > Parkinson's disease management in a younger person is difficult. The Parkinson's condition will magnify symptomatology from other causes. We call these "non-motor complications" of Parkinson's disease. This includes pain.

CLIN-003242  > As a simple analogy if someone had had a serious back injury and was left in chronic pain — we would expect them to have a much worse experience of their pain, because of their Parkinson's disease especially when the effect of the Levodopa Sinemet tablets are wearing off.

CLIN-003243  > In particular as [Patient] noted, when he has the effects of the Sinemet tablets, there is less experience of pain. This is the older Sinemet effects and also the recent Sinemet controlled release that he has talked of in his own account and as was noted.

CLIN-003244  > Of itself, we do not think that intrinsically Parkinson's disease directly "causes all pain" that a person with Parkinson's disease may have. But as above it will most certainly magnify the experience of pain — of whatever cause.

CLIN-003245  > However, Parkinson's disease management with more and more Sinemet becomes especially difficult in the setting of a younger person. We will commonly quote a risk of 50% of patients on Levodopa treatment developing troublesome dyskinesias (abnormal excessive movements) by about 5 years of treatment, and there are other significant and serious complications including Dopamine Dysregulation Syndrome.

CLIN-003246  > These problems are more prominent in younger people, who will be left with these consequences for longer periods of time. This is why I have been careful and slow in increasing his treatment.

CLIN-003247  > I fully accept and respect [Patient]'s account of things. However, [Patient] has been on treatment with Sinemet for quite some time. As the letters show, we have had a clear understanding for quite some time that Sinemet does help his pain.

CLIN-003248  > My reluctance to increase his Sinemet treatment quickly was because of concerns of evolution of the dyskinesia and obviously the other risks.

CLIN-003249  > "If there is a degree of peripheral neuropathy here, the alcohol being gone will hopefully slow the deterioration of that."

CLIN-003250  > As can be seen we are not blaming the alcohol to the exclusion of the consideration of any other cause.

CLIN-003251  > "He knows very well that once the Sinemet hits his system that the feet feel better from a pain perspective. That is commonly described. Sinemet should not do anything for pain but it will relieve the experience of pain to at least some degree. For example if he had had old serious ankle or leg fractures that were still playing up Sinemet would probably make them feel better as well."

CLIN-003252  > As can be seen we have completely accepted the beneficial effect that a person with Parkinson's may have on the experience of pain of whatever cause, from Sinemet. But this is couched in concerns of the sustainability of the benefit that might be achieved and the risks of side effects especially in the longer term, especially in a younger person.

CLIN-003253  > [Patient] explains his concern that everything has been framed as an "alcohol related peripheral neuropathy". I do accept the different perceptions of the person and the provider, but I think that everything has been very clearly framed in the larger context of our concern and understanding of his Parkinson's disorder.

CLIN-003254  > From the very outset the majority of everything that has been done has been about [Patient]'s Parkinson's. For my own account, I can only say that I always tried to do what I felt was right and appropriate at a given point in time, and to gently and sensitively communicate that.

CLIN-003255  > We have had a second opinion for [Patient] in the very early stages of his condition, from Professor Daniel Healy, a noted Parkinson's Disease expert in Dublin at Beaumont Hospital.

CLIN-003256  > When [Patient] explained his concerns in recent years that he did not really have Parkinson's disease, I have tried to oblige him by requesting another isotope DAT scan, even when I believed that he did have Parkinson's disease.

CLIN-003257  > I have tried to answer and explore whatever concerns he has had. I have always completely accepted that I may be wrong.

CLIN-003258  > It was a reasonable possibility that alcohol intake at the amounts described may have been in some way contributory to the neuropathic symptoms. Because Parkinson's disease is not ordinarily for the vast majority of people a condition with prominent features of neuropathic pain, it is reasonable to question that there was another cause for the severity of the pain [Patient] has experienced.

CLIN-003259  > Where [Patient] explains he independently started Sinemet CR in March 2025, I'm not certain if this was separate to my own prescription for Sinemet CR in March 2025 two weeks ago or by another doctor/consultant.

CLIN-003260  > In 2017 very openly and very explicitly the following passage was written and discussed very clearly, at the very beginning:

CLIN-003261  > "[Patient] has a good understanding of why we wished to delay the commencement of levodopa or dopamine agonist type treatments at least for now."

CLIN-003262  > "I think sooner or later we may have to introduce these. It is possible that he will do very well with levodopa and in a sustainable way whenever it is started. Indeed in time to come — we may regret that we did not start this earlier. However you will understand our delay at the moment as part of the strategy to try to make sure that [Patient] has the best possible long-term outcome."

CLIN-003263  > In essence we have known that [Patient] has Parkinson's; we have known it would be Sinemet or other dopamine agonist responsive; we have tried to spare commencing those for some time as is common practice in the management of younger people with Parkinson's.

CLIN-003264  > He has been on Sinemet treatment for some time and we have accepted certain consequence of that which we have managed; it has been known and acknowledged that Sinemet helps his experience of ongoing pain.

CLIN-003265  > His Lyrica pregabalin treatment was introduced for the neuropathic pain, of whatever cause, in part to try to spare further Sinemet increase; his Lyrica treatment, admittedly with incomplete effect at maximum dose, was self-reduced; his pain deteriorated.

CLIN-003266  > Now his Sinemet treatment has been recently escalated at clinic, with a plan from clinic to do this slowly and carefully as Sinemet CR, which has provided him with profound relief currently.

CLIN-003267  > In answer to his question, "This raises a fundamental question: Why was this possibility not explored earlier, given the magnitude of what Liam endured?", [Patient] has been on forms of Sinemet since September 2021 for the range of symptoms that Parkinson's is associated with.

CLIN-003268  > We have tried to be careful in how this is used, and how much is used, given difficulties with previous forms of Sinemet used (Stalevo), given his young age, and given risks of dyskinesias and Dopamine Dysregulation/Impulse Control Disorders described in people with Parkinson's on these agents.

CLIN-003269  > As a final note I am very, very pleased that [Patient] has had such an improvement with the Sinemet CR medication, because I can only begin to try to imagine all he had gone through in terms of pain — as he had so clearly, vividly and well described as per my letter of earlier March 2025.

CLIN-003270  > I do most sincerely hope this will be sustained and be without adverse effect.

CLIN-003271  > Once again I am sincerely sorry on a professional, but also very deeply on a personal level that this has been [Patient]'s experience.

CLIN-003272  **Signed:** Dr Thomas Monaghan MB MRCPI
CLIN-003273  **Role:** Consultant Neurologist

CLIN-003274  # DOCUMENT 22 — Quality and Patient Safety Letter — 2025-04-15

CLIN-003275  # Quality and Patient Safety Department — Galway University Hospital

CLIN-003276  **Source:** One-page PDF supplied directly by the patient.
CLIN-003277  **Document type:** Complaint-response correspondence.
CLIN-003278  **Date:** 15/04/2025
CLIN-003279  **Author:** Aoife Nolan, Quality & Patient Safety Coordinator and Complaints Officer
CLIN-003280  **Transcription status:** Substantive transcription from supplied PDF. Patient identifiers and non-essential private details have been generalised.

CLIN-003281  ## Letter

CLIN-003282  > I refer to your complaint received by the Quality and Patient Safety Department on the 25th of March in relation to concerns of bias and diagnostic anchoring, that you feel affected how your condition was managed, in particular in relation to pain management.

CLIN-003283  > You recently started Sinemet CR, which has been an effective analgesic for your foot pain.

CLIN-003284  > However, you feel this particular medication was not considered by your Neurologist, possibly due to an emphasis on your history of alcohol excess, which led to prolonged and preventable pain.

CLIN-003285  > When a complaint is received by the Complaints Officer and following its assessment is determined to relate to matters solely relating to clinical judgment, it must be sent to the appropriate manager for follow up under the appropriate pathway in accordance with the Health Act, 2004.

CLIN-003286  > In this regard, your complaint was forwarded for the attention of Consultant Neurologist, Dr. Thomas Monaghan.

CLIN-003287  > Thank you for bringing your concerns to our attention and for the opportunity to respond. We endeavour to improve our service at every opportunity to ensure we provide a high quality service to those in our care.

CLIN-003288  **Signed:** Aoife Nolan
CLIN-003289  **Role:** Quality & Patient Safety Coordinator and Complaints Officer

CLIN-003439  # DOCUMENT 25 — Formal Complaint — Diagnostic Anchoring, Bias, and Failure to Reassess

CLIN-003440  **Date / period:** 19 March 2025
CLIN-003441  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient-authored complaint
CLIN-003442  **Provenance:** Connected Gmail message 195ae59e7b76c054; original attachment Formal complaint.docx
CLIN-003443  **Forensic status:** Primary contemporaneous notice/discovery document: proves what the patient knew, believed and asked the institution to investigate on 19 March 2025.
CLIN-003444  **Source-status note:** The source preserves the patient’s own March formulations, including “independently trialed” and claims that the response “effectively” disproved a prior working diagnosis. Later records show Sinemet CR was prescribed by Neurology on 10 March and that earlier dopaminergic/anticholinergic treatment history existed. Those later refinements do not alter what the complaint actually said at the time.

CLIN-003445  ## Gmail transmission

CLIN-003446  **Gmail message ID:** `195ae59e7b76c054`

CLIN-003447  The complaint was sent on 19 March 2025 to GUH Quality & Patient Safety / Your Service Your Say with the attached DOCX. The transmission requested independent review, disclosure, explanation and preservation.

CLIN-003448  ## Attached complaint — extracted from the original DOCX

CLIN-003449  Formal Complaint – Diagnostic Anchoring, Bias, and Failure to Reassess (2017–2025)

CLIN-003450  To Whom It May Concern,

CLIN-003451  My name is Liam Moloney, DOB 12/11/1977, Patient ID 0360424.

CLIN-003452  I am writing to raise serious concerns regarding the clinical handling of my case by Galway East Medical Practice (Dr. Aaron Leo Brennan) and the Neurology Department at Galway University Hospitals (Dr. Thomas Monaghan) during the period 2017 to 2025.

CLIN-003453  I submit this complaint with respect for the complexity of clinical decision-making, but also with a sincere request for accountability, transparency, and learning—both in relation to my case and potentially others affected by similar processes.



CLIN-003454  Background

CLIN-003455  My presenting symptom—sharp, pressure-sensitive foot pain—began in 2005. By 2017, I sought medical intervention through Galway East Medical Practice.

CLIN-003456  From that point, the pain was consistently attributed to peripheral neuropathy, likely alcohol-related, and this framing appears to have followed me into Neurology referrals and specialist reviews.

CLIN-003457  For eight years, despite continued reporting of pain and its impact, the working diagnosis did not materially change, nor was it re-evaluated against emerging symptoms or alternative explanations, including Parkinson's-related pain syndromes or dopamine-responsive conditions.



CLIN-003458  Concerns Raised

CLIN-003459  I make this complaint fully aware that I do not have access to every internal note, referral, or correspondence generated over these years. However, I am now left with deep concern that the following occurred, or that the system made it impossible to detect if they did:

CLIN-003460  Diagnostic Anchoring and Bias


CLIN-003461  Early framing of my condition as alcohol-related neuropathy became fixed, potentially precluding further investigation.

CLIN-003462  Failure to Reassess


CLIN-003463  Despite deterioration, ongoing pain, and functional impairment, no fresh neurological workup was undertaken that considered Parkinson’s-related pain syndromes or tested dopaminergic responsiveness.

CLIN-003464  Potential Hierarchical Influence


CLIN-003465  I have reason to believe senior clinical influence may have advised caution against certain treatments, which—combined with referral bias—closed off viable therapeutic trials.



CLIN-003466  The Turning Point

CLIN-003467  In March 2025, I independently trialed Sinemet CR. The result was immediate and sustained relief from two decades of disabling foot pain—effectively disproving the prior working diagnosis and suggesting my condition was dopamine-responsive all along.

CLIN-003468  This raises a fundamental question: Why was this possibility not explored earlier, given the magnitude of harm endured?



CLIN-003469  The Harm Caused

CLIN-003470  Physical: Prolonged, preventable pain

CLIN-003471  Psychological: Fear of movement, cumulative distress, and erosion of resilience

CLIN-003472  Loss of Trust: A profound impact on my confidence in clinical systems' ability to self-correct



CLIN-003473  Request for Review and Disclosure

CLIN-003474  I make no presumptions about individual intent or malice. My concern is systemic: that processes of diagnostic framing, confirmation bias, and deference to hierarchy may have overridden patient experience and clinical curiosity.

CLIN-003475  I respectfully request:

CLIN-003476  An independent review of my case, with attention to the clinical reasoning applied from 2017 to 2025.

CLIN-003477  Full disclosure of my medical records, referrals, specialist correspondence, and any internal memos related to my case during this period.

CLIN-003478  A written response explaining how this outcome was possible, and what measures exist to prevent similar diagnostic anchoring from persisting unchecked in other cases.

CLIN-003479  Preservation of all records related to my case, including internal communications, pending my full review.



CLIN-003480  Closing Statement

CLIN-003481  I submit this complaint not in anger, but from a place of humility and genuine need for understanding.

CLIN-003482  I recognise the pressures and complexities of medical practice. However, after eight years of persistent pain and the eventual discovery that relief was possible, I am compelled to ask how the system could have missed this—despite my continued engagement.

CLIN-003483  My aim is not confrontation, but to ensure learning, accountability, and the protection of future patients from similar harm.



CLIN-003484  Yours sincerely,

CLIN-003807        specialist. Despite this admission, he continued to manage a complex case of
CLIN-003808        Parkinsonism — including textbook features and a positive DAT scan — for more
CLIN-003809        than eight years without subspecialist referral, dopaminergic treatment, or
CLIN-003810        diagnostic escalation.
CLIN-003811     ●​ This document outlines the legal, clinical, and ethical obligations triggered by that
CLIN-003812        admission, and the consequences of failing to act on them. It is not a critique of
CLIN-003813        clinical judgment alone — it is an examination of professional overreach,
CLIN-003814        diagnostic anchoring, and institutional inaction following a clearly stated
CLIN-003815        scope-of-practice limitation.
CLIN-003816     ●​ The harm that followed was not abstract. It was lived daily through misdiagnosed,
CLIN-003817        untreated Parkinsonian dystonia that resolved instantly when the patient initiated
CLIN-003818        standard therapy himself. This is not a theoretical argument. It is a matter of
CLIN-003819        record. The admission occurred. The obligations existed. The duty was ignored.
CLIN-003820     ●​ This document now presents the consequences.




CLIN-003821       🔶 II. The Core Admission (Primary Evidence)
CLIN-003822  In a clinic letter dated July 2017, written by Dr. Thomas Monaghan to the referring GP, the
CLIN-003823  following statement appears:

CLIN-003824       “In reality, while I’m not an expert on these conditions (I am not a movement
CLIN-003825       disorders neurologist)…”
CLIN-003826  This sentence stands as the most significant clinical and legal inflection point in the
CLIN-003827  case. It is not vague. It is not inferred. It is explicit. The treating neurologist declared his
CLIN-003828  lack of expertise in the very subspecialty most relevant to the patient’s diagnosis and
CLIN-003829  ongoing care: movement disorders neurology.

CLIN-003830     Contextual Details:

CLIN-003831     ●​ At the time of this admission, the working diagnosis was Parkinsonism, as
CLIN-003832        acknowledged elsewhere in the same communication.​

CLIN-003833     ●​ The patient presented with chronic, progressive, dopamine-responsive dystonia, a
CLIN-003834        symptom profile well within the domain of movement disorders.​

CLIN-003835     ●​ Despite the admission, no referral to a movement disorders specialist was
CLIN-003836        initiated then or in the eight years that followed.​

CLIN-003837     ●​ All therapeutic and diagnostic decisions remained under the care of the admitting
CLIN-003838        neurologist, with no external consultation, re-evaluation, or second opinion
CLIN-003839        sought.​



CLIN-003840     Why This Matters:

CLIN-003841  This statement is not a footnote. It is an invocation of professional limitation — and
CLIN-003842  under both ethical guidelines and clinical duty of care standards, it mandates a referral or
CLIN-003843  escalation. Instead, the admission was buried inside a routine clinic note, and no action
CLIN-003844  followed.

CLIN-003845  The moment a clinician articulates that they are operating outside their scope of
CLIN-003846  subspecialist training, every subsequent decision they make in that area becomes legally
CLIN-003847  and ethically exposed. From this point forward, clinical harm became foreseeable,
CLIN-003848  preventable, and institutionally traceable.




CLIN-003849        🧭 III. Professional and Legal Obligations
CLIN-003850        Triggered
CLIN-003851  The moment Dr. Monaghan declared he was “not a movement disorders neurologist,” a
CLIN-003852  series of professional responsibilities became active — as defined by the Irish Medical
CLIN-003853  Council’s Guide to Professional Conduct and Ethics for Registered Medical Practitioners.
CLIN-003854  These are not advisory suggestions. They are binding standards.

CLIN-003856        Obligation          Code                          Description of Duty
CLIN-003857                          Reference



CLIN-003858   Recognise and act     §2.1             “You must recognise and work within the limits of
CLIN-003859   within your limits                     your competence and refer a patient to another
CLIN-003860                                          doctor when it is in the patient’s best interests.”



CLIN-003861   Refer when outside    §2.2             “You must refer a patient to a colleague with
CLIN-003862   scope                                  appropriate expertise when this is necessary for
CLIN-003863                                          the patient’s care.”



CLIN-003864   Respect patient       §3.2             “You should work in partnership with your patients
CLIN-003865   insight and                            and respect their insights into their condition.”
CLIN-003866   partnership




CLIN-003867  Implications of These Clauses:

CLIN-003868     1.​ §2.1 was triggered the moment the clinician stated he was not a movement
CLIN-003869         disorders specialist. He was now obliged to stop or share care.​

CLIN-003870     2.​ §2.2 required that he refer the patient to a colleague with subspecialty training —
CLIN-003871         i.e., a movement disorders neurologist.​

CLIN-003872     3.​ §3.2 was violated repeatedly, as the patient’s ongoing reports of severe,
CLIN-003873         progressive pain and the eventual dopamine-responsiveness of symptoms were
CLIN-003874         not explored, validated, or followed up with diagnostic reassessment.​


CLIN-003875       The failure to uphold these standards is not discretionary. It constitutes a

CLIN-003877       negligence.
CLIN-003878       🚫 IV. What Was Not Done

CLIN-003880  Monaghan — and by extension, the institution overseeing the case — failed to take any

CLIN-003913  place to:

CLIN-003988  🔍 A. The Admission Was the Moment the Clock Started
CLIN-003989        “I am not a movement disorders neurologist.”

CLIN-003993  📉 B. This Was Not Caution — It Was Negligence

CLIN-004198  **Forensic status:** Primary evidence of the patient’s developing procedural theory and insistence on traceable written process.
CLIN-004199  **Source-status note:** Claims that the classification was containment, structural deflection, or misrepresentation are the patient’s assertions. The source directly establishes that by 12 May he was treating procedural classification and documentary traceability as part of the dispute.

CLIN-004200  Ms. Nolan,

CLIN-004201  This letter serves as a formal procedural statement concerning the complaint I submitted and your role in its assessment and classification.

CLIN-004202  You demonstrated a clear and accurate understanding of my complaint — its structure, clinical implications, and stated harm. Your summary of the issues was more coherent than any response that followed. That is precisely what makes the subsequent classification — as a matter of clinical judgment — irreconcilable.

CLIN-004203  You applied the term “assessment” to a process that resulted in the rerouting of a systemic and evidence-supported complaint back to the subject of that complaint. In doing so, the complaint was diverted from scrutiny and formal review. The word “assessment” here was not just procedural shorthand — it functioned as a decisive instrument of containment.

CLIN-004204  Your invocation of “assessment” implies a rigorous process. If such a process took place, it must now be disclosed. If no such documentation exists, the use of that term represents procedural misrepresentation.

CLIN-004205  Procedural Documentation and Continuity

CLIN-004206  For procedural continuity, the following communications form the documented chain of correspondence:
CLIN-004207  - April 18 – Request to Supplement Complaint File
CLIN-004208  - April 20 – Tier III Submission and Request for Support
CLIN-004209  - April 26 – Formal Resubmission of Complaint
CLIN-004210  - April 28 – Direct Outreach to Prof. Counihan

CLIN-004211  As of today, 62 days have passed since the therapeutic reversal that contradicted the system’s working diagnosis. In that time — while recovering from untreated neurological pain, self-managing a new medication regimen, and processing the implications of long-term diagnostic failure — I have authored and submitted a structurally complete complaint; produced a comprehensive archive; initiated contact with clinical, regulatory and oversight bodies; and documented each stage of silence, misdirection or refusal to engage.

CLIN-004212  From this point forward, all correspondence is to occur exclusively via email to ensure traceability.

CLIN-004213  Yours sincerely,
CLIN-004214  Liam Moloney
CLIN-004215  Patient Advocate
CLIN-004216  Public Witness

CLIN-004297  Dear Emily,

CLIN-004298  Thank you for your recent update. I now need to formally set the record straight — for internal governance purposes and to prevent further procedural mischaracterisation.

CLIN-004299  On 28 March 2025 at 11:07am, I received a phone call from your office. That call lasted 6 minutes and 43 seconds. During that call, I gave a clear and direct instruction: that my complaint, received and logged by QPSD on 25 March 2025, be escalated and processed as a Stage 2 complaint under the HSE “Your Service Your Say” process.

CLIN-004300  Despite that instruction, the complaint was reclassified as “a matter of clinical judgment”, routed to Dr. Monaghan for reply, and a closure letter was issued on 15 April with no reference to the call or escalation request.

CLIN-004301  Clarification on the Nature of My Submission

CLIN-004302  The complaint I submitted in March 2025 was not “evidenced” in legal terms because I had no access to internal documentation.

CLIN-004303  It was a first-stage disclosure of harm and diagnostic contradiction — which is precisely what the Stage 2 process exists to evaluate.

CLIN-004304  Had my request to escalate been followed, your office would have discovered the internal documents already on file.

CLIN-004305  Instead, I obtained them independently — through FOI — and now the evidence supports my position beyond doubt.

CLIN-004306  This is not a fresh complaint. It is a formal correction of record — supported by timestamped evidence — concerning how a valid escalation was ignored, a complaint misrouted, and procedural safeguards bypassed.

CLIN-004307  [The email then lists requested corrective actions and preservation requests.]

CLIN-004356  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-004357  I am writing under Article 16 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) to formally request rectification and annotation of inaccurate personal data held about me within Galway University Hospitals.

CLIN-004358  Patient Details:

CLIN-004359  Name: Liam Moloney

CLIN-004360  DOB: 12/11/1977

CLIN-004361  Patient ID: 0360424

CLIN-004362  Between 2017 and 2025, I was under the care of your Neurology Department, where I was assigned a diagnosis of idiopathic Parkinson’s disease. However, this diagnosis was reached without incorporating or exploring my primary presenting symptom: bilateral foot pain, which began in 2004–2005 and was the basis for my GP referral in 2017.

CLIN-004363  In 2025, following my own adjustment of dopaminergic medication (Sinemet CR), I experienced immediate and sustained relief of that symptom — effectively disproving the previously presumed peripheral neuropathy and revealing that the symptom was dopamine-responsive all along.

CLIN-004364  This finding invalidates critical assumptions underpinning the diagnosis recorded and disseminated across my medical history.

CLIN-004365  GDPR Grounds:

CLIN-004366  Under Article 16 of GDPR, I am entitled to request rectification where my data is:

CLIN-004367  Inaccurate (e.g., omission of key symptom in referral/consultation notes),

CLIN-004368  Misleading (e.g., implication of peripheral neuropathy without confirmatory testing),

CLIN-004369  Or incomplete in a clinically significant way (e.g., diagnosis recorded as conclusive despite absence of diagnostic levodopa trial).

CLIN-004370  The long-standing diagnostic label in my file — and its downstream replication — does not reflect the full, accurate clinical picture, especially now that a dopamine-responsive mechanism has been demonstrated.

CLIN-004371  Requested Actions:

CLIN-004372  1. Rectify all relevant documents where the absence of my core symptom (severe, disabling foot pain) misrepresents my diagnostic journey.

CLIN-004373  2. Annotate all files (referrals, discharge summaries, diagnostic letters) that present a conclusive Parkinson’s diagnosis, noting the lack of dopaminergic diagnostic trial and the subsequent contradictory evidence in 2025.

CLIN-004374  3. Preserve all internal and external records relating to my case for further review, including MDT notes, email correspondence, and meeting records.

CLIN-004375  This request is made in good faith, in pursuit of factual accuracy and the correction of records that continue to influence how my health condition is understood, managed, and recorded.

CLIN-004376  Please confirm receipt of this request and respond within the statutory time frame of one calendar month.

CLIN-004377  Sincerely,

CLIN-004378  Liam Moloney

CLIN-004379  [home address redacted]

CLIN-004380  [patient phone redacted]

CLIN-004398  Dear Mr. Moloney,


CLIN-004399        I have received communication arising from the Office of the Ombudsman in
CLIN-004400        relation to apparent concerns which you have raised about the management of your
CLIN-004401        complaint, which was first received by the Quality and Patient Safety Department
CLIN-004402        on the 25ᵗʰ of March 2025.


CLIN-004403        I understand you have expressed concern with regard to the objectivity of the
CLIN-004404        hospital’s approach to the management of your complaint. The purpose of my
CLIN-004405        correspondence to you today is to provide you with additional clarity with regard to
CLIN-004406        how complaints are managed.


CLIN-004407        The HSE Your Service Your Say (YSYS) Policy 2017 is underpinned by Part 9 of
CLIN-004408        the 2004 Health Act and 2006 Complaints Regulations. This legislation exempts
CLIN-004409        certain matters for examination under the YSYS policy. Section 45 (17) outlines
CLIN-004410        that a person is not entitled to make a complaint about a matter relating solely to
CLIN-004411        the exercise of clinical judgement by a person acting on behalf of the Executive
CLIN-004412        (HSE).


CLIN-004413        As outlined in the letter of response issued on the 15ᵗʰ of April, a complaint that
CLIN-004414        relates to matters of clinical judgement is always made available to the clinician
CLIN-004415        whom the complaint is about, for their observations and response where
CLIN-004416        appropriate. This is to afford the clinician an opportunity to respond to what has
CLIN-004417        been alleged. Please note therefore that the role of a Complaints Officer in the case
CLIN-004418        of matters of clinical judgement is not to investigate the matter, but to simply
CLIN-004419        coordinate the management of the complaint. This involves ensuring that the
CLIN-004420        complaint is received by the correct clinician, and that a response is provided back
CLIN-004421        to the complainant. It is important to emphasise here that as matters of clinical
CLIN-004422        judgement are exempt from examination under the YSYS policy, they are not
CLIN-004423        subject to a Stage 3 and Stage 4 Review.


CLIN-004424        As you were not reassured by the response provided to you on 25ᵗʰ April, I
CLIN-004425        consulted with the Clinical Director for Medicine in GUH, who agreed to have
CLIN-004426        your complaint reviewed independently by the hospital’s incident management team
CLIN-004427        to allay your concerns about transparency. You were advised of this in a letter
CLIN-004428        issued to you on the 30ᵗʰ of May.


CLIN-004429        YSYS​
CLIN-004430        My role is to ensure a complaint that relates to a clinical issue is referred to the
CLIN-004431        respective clinician who provided care, and to issue this response to the
CLIN-004432        complainant in due course.


CLIN-004433        Incident Management Framework (IMF)​
CLIN-004434        Your complaint will be reviewed independently by an incident management team in
CLIN-004435        accordance with this framework.




CLIN-004436        I wish to thank you for your correspondence to date and the information which you
CLIN-004437        have provided. Given the volume of correspondence received to date in relation to
CLIN-004438        your complaint, and my commitment to a high volume of current open cases within
CLIN-004439        my remit, I will require some time to review, collate and coordinate your complaint.
CLIN-004440        In that regard, I ask that you kindly refrain from forwarding me any further
CLIN-004441        correspondence and documentation at this time as this will only deter from
CLIN-004442        advancing and progressing your complaint. I undertake to revert to you once the
CLIN-004443        relevant personnel have reviewed your case and a response has been
CLIN-004444        prepared.


CLIN-004445        I hope my correspondence has provided you with more clarity on the process and
CLIN-004446        assurance that I play no part in the examination and further review of your
CLIN-004447        complaint.


CLIN-004448  Yours sincerely,


CLIN-004449  (signature)​
CLIN-004450  Aoife Nolan​

CLIN-004498  SUBJECT: GDPR Art. 16 & Health Act 2004 Rectification Request — QPS “Closure Letter” Template
CLIN-004499  TIMESTAMP: 2025-06-15 11:36:58 UTC; equivalent to 2025-06-15 12:36:58 Europe/Dublin.
CLIN-004500  ATTACHMENT: Exhibit A.jpg. The attachment image is not transcribed in this file because the requested item is the primary correspondence text. Its existence and filename are verified from Gmail.

CLIN-004501  COMPLETE SUBSTANTIVE TEXT

CLIN-004502  Dear Data Protection Officer and Dean of Medical Education,

CLIN-004503  I write under Article 16 GDPR and Section 59 of Ireland’s Data Protection Act 2018 to request the immediate rectification of all Quality & Patient Safety (QPS) “closure letters” issued by Galway University Hospital that:

CLIN-004504  -

CLIN-004505  Invoke the Health Act 2004 as justification for rerouting complaints back to the original clinician; and

CLIN-004506  -

CLIN-004507  Use the term “assessment” to describe an undocumented, non-transparent internal process that classifies complaints as “solely clinical” without independent review or appeal.

CLIN-004508  Problem Definition

CLIN-004509  By “assessment,” I refer specifically to the procedural device used by QPS to unilaterally categorise complaints as exempt from broader governance review, particularly where concerns involve diagnostic bias, ethical error, or systemic failure. This language — presented as objective — masks a lack of formal process, independent input, or appeal mechanism.

CLIN-004510  Similarly, reliance on the Health Act 2004 in such letters is legally inaccurate. Section 28(4)(b) of the Act requires that complaints be dealt with “in an appropriate manner,” which cannot be satisfied by recirculation to the clinician originally implicated — particularly when the complaint raises issues of ethical oversight or reasoning error.

CLIN-004511  Relevance to Teaching Hospital Standards

CLIN-004512  In a university teaching hospital, such as GUH, these procedural practices become institutionally self-replicating:

CLIN-004513  -

CLIN-004514  They are absorbed into clinical governance education, complaint-handling training, and student-facing materials.

CLIN-004515  -

CLIN-004516  They model poor legal and procedural reasoning for future clinicians.

CLIN-004517  -

CLIN-004518  They risk embedding misconceptions of fair process and patient rights into the medical training ecosystem.

CLIN-004519  As such, these letters — when reused in M&M rounds, e-learning modules, or internal staff guidance — violate GDPR provisions concerning data use in education and undermine ethical standards for clinical teaching.

CLIN-004520  GDPR Breaches

CLIN-004521  Re-use or circulation of such letters, without correction, constitutes a breach of:

CLIN-004522  -

CLIN-004523  Art. 5(1)(b) – Purpose Limitation: These documents were created for administrative closure, not teaching;

CLIN-004524  -

CLIN-004525  Art. 5(1)(d) – Accuracy: They contain legally incorrect references to the Health Act and misrepresent complaint procedures;

CLIN-004526  -

CLIN-004527  Art. 5(2) – Accountability: The institution must demonstrate correction of such errors;

CLIN-004528  -

CLIN-004529  Art. 89 – Safeguards for educational reuse of personal data.

CLIN-004530  The inaccurate classification of my own complaint under “assessment” creates a material distortion of my patient record. As this data is accessible to staff and may inform governance processes or future training, it directly violates Article 5(1)(d) GDPR and warrants correction.

CLIN-004531  Formal Request

CLIN-004532  Accordingly, I request that within 30 days you:

CLIN-004533  -

CLIN-004534  Identify all instances — in records and educational materials — where the closure-letter template containing both “assessment” and Health Act invocation appears. This includes:

CLIN-004535  -

CLIN-004536  Complaint response letters

CLIN-004537  -

CLIN-004538  Clinical governance teaching materials

CLIN-004539  -

CLIN-004540  M&M rounds, training modules, or e-learning platforms

CLIN-004541  -

CLIN-004542  Rectify each instance by appending this annotation:

CLIN-004543  “This document contains a legally inaccurate use of the term ‘assessment’ and a misapplication of the Health Act 2004. A GDPR rectification request (Art. 16) is pending.”

CLIN-004544  -

CLIN-004545  Withdraw or annotate all uses of this template in educational or secondary contexts, including clinical staff workshops.

CLIN-004546  -

CLIN-004547  Provide an audit log confirming, for each instance:

CLIN-004548  -

CLIN-004549  Whether it has been corrected or withdrawn;

CLIN-004550  -

CLIN-004551  The date of correction;

CLIN-004552  -

CLIN-004553  That future reuse will not occur without explicit review and reauthorization.

CLIN-004554  -

CLIN-004555  Report the following statistics:

CLIN-004556  -

CLIN-004557  The % of all QPS closure letters since 2020 using the “assessment + Health Act” formulation;

CLIN-004558  -

CLIN-004559  The % of internal or external training sessions incorporating such material;

CLIN-004560  -

CLIN-004561  Any internal QPSD metrics relating to classification accuracy or independent complaint review.

CLIN-004562  -

CLIN-004563  Confirm whether any internal safeguards or document review processes are in place to prevent this legal misrepresentation from continuing in governance or teaching materials.

CLIN-004564  In a teaching hospital, the language used to close complaints is not merely administrative — it models standards of fairness, candour, and legal clarity for emerging clinicians. Procedural shortcuts masked by legal misinterpretation cannot form the basis of ethical clinical governance or education.

CLIN-004565  Failure to comply will necessitate referral to:

CLIN-004566  -

CLIN-004567  The Data Protection Commission (for breaches of Articles 5(1)(b), 5(1)(d), 5(2), 16, and 89 GDPR);

CLIN-004568  -

CLIN-004569  The Medical Council, for professional and educational governance failure.

CLIN-004570  Attached as Exhibit A is the “Nolan Closure Letter – 15 April 2025,” which exemplifies the procedural misclassification and legal misstatement at issue. I request rectification of this and all materially similar documents.

CLIN-004571  Please confirm receipt and provide full details of all actions taken.

CLIN-004611  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-004612  Under Article 15 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), I am requesting access to all personal data and institutional documentation relating to a bedside teaching interaction that took place during my inpatient admission to St. Anthony’s Ward, Galway University Hospital, on or around 10–11 July 2017 (Patient ID: 0360424).

CLIN-004613  On one of these dates, I was the subject of a teaching encounter involving four medical trainees, conducted within the institutional framework of the hospital’s medical education programme.

CLIN-004614  During this interaction:

CLIN-004615  -

CLIN-004616  I raised concern that my core presenting symptom — disabling bilateral foot pain — had not been addressed.

CLIN-004617  -

CLIN-004618  I requested that one of the students fetch the supervising neurologist.

CLIN-004619  -

CLIN-004620  In the presence of all four trainees, I said:

CLIN-004621  “So are you telling me you’re going to bring me in here, give me the full NCT, and send me out on bald tires?”

CLIN-004622  -

CLIN-004623  The consultant’s response, paraphrased, was:

CLIN-004624  “It’s as easy for you to get a podiatry appointment as it is for me.”

CLIN-004625  I believe this moment was either recorded or witnessed in a manner consistent with the hospital’s responsibilities as a clinical teaching institution. Regardless of whether the interaction was formally documented, it occurred under hospital governance, supervision, and educational oversight.

CLIN-004626  Accordingly, I request access to:

CLIN-004627  -

CLIN-004628  All clinical documentation authored on 10–11 July 2017 related to this event or referencing student involvement

CLIN-004629  -

CLIN-004630  Any student logbooks, observation records, or teaching notes generated as part of this interaction

CLIN-004631  -

CLIN-004632  Ward round records, consultant entries, or trainee annotations that may reference my symptoms, foot pain, or the teaching interaction

CLIN-004633  -

CLIN-004634  Any associated governance, supervisory, or education documents describing bedside teaching in St. Anthony’s Ward during this period

CLIN-004635  -

CLIN-004636  Trainee assignment records or rosters identifying students present on my case during that week

CLIN-004637  If any material contains third-party information, I request appropriate redaction rather than refusal, in accordance with Article 15(4) GDPR and proportionality standards.

CLIN-004638  This request is made in good faith, and in pursuit of understanding how an unaddressed clinical concern — one I flagged clearly — was modeled as a resolved interaction in the presence of trainees. It represents a case of educational culture that has had direct and enduring consequences for my care.

CLIN-004639  I ask that you confirm receipt of this request and respond within the statutory one-month timeframe.

CLIN-004744  COMPLETE SUBSTANTIVE TEXT

CLIN-004745  Dear Ms. Madden,

CLIN-004746  Thank you for your message of 14 July 2025.

CLIN-004747  Let me begin by stating unequivocally: this is not personal. I do not direct blame at you as an individual, and I acknowledge that you operate within an institutional framework not of your own making.

CLIN-004748  However, you are a named representative of an institution that now has a documented record of confusing statutory rights, misapplying legal terms, and breaching procedural obligations under the General Data Protection Regulation. That must be addressed directly.

CLIN-004749  Your message—meant as a receipt—has instead produced a procedural collapse. It refers vaguely to “rectification,” does not specify which request, omits reference to any statutory timeline, and arrives after at least one legal deadline has already expired. What should have been a simple confirmation has become a source of confusion more befitting a contested contract than a lawful GDPR reply.

CLIN-004750  While Galway University Hospital may be content to populate its internal documentation with conflation, obfuscation, and pre-emptive disclaimers, that standard has no place in data protection law. It does not meet the requirements of the General Data Protection Regulation, and it does not work for me.

CLIN-004751  Let us begin with what should be a very simple matter of interpretation:

CLIN-004752  If by “your request for rectification” you meant my Article 15 access request submitted on 7 July 2025

CLIN-004753  Then your reply constitutes a material legal misstatement. An access request is not a rectification request. These are distinct legal rights with separate grounds, timelines, and obligations. Mislabeling one as the other undermines confidence in your institution’s ability to process either.

CLIN-004754  If you meant instead my Article 16 rectification request of 12 June 2025

CLIN-004755  Then your message arrived two days after the legal deadline, without any Article 12(3) extension, justification, or acknowledgment of breach. That failure is complete.

CLIN-004756  If you meant the Article 16 rectification request of 15 June 2025

CLIN-004757  Then your response was issued one day before the deadline, with no substantive acknowledgment, no confirmation of processing, and no indication that the request had been formally distinguished from the others.

CLIN-004758  Legal Questions Requiring Immediate and Unambiguous Answers:

CLIN-004759  -

CLIN-004760  Has your office formally logged and distinguished between the three GDPR requests I have submitted (12 June, 15 June, 7 July)?

CLIN-004761  -

CLIN-004762  Was any Article 12(3) extension notice issued in respect of the 12 June rectification request before the statutory deadline of 12 July expired?

CLIN-004763  -

CLIN-004764  Who is responsible for ensuring each request is processed in line with the correct GDPR Article?

CLIN-004765  -

CLIN-004766  Will I receive written confirmation of whether my medical record will be corrected, annotated, or left unchanged—and on what basis?

CLIN-004767  -

CLIN-004768  Do you accept that failure to correctly identify the nature of a request in your acknowledgment constitutes a breach of GDPR’s transparency and accountability obligations?

CLIN-004769  -

CLIN-004770  Do you acknowledge that referring to a subject access request as “rectification” is a misstatement of law?

CLIN-004771  I would prefer not to escalate this further. But if clarity is not provided immediately—and if misstatements of law persist—I will refer this matter in full to the Data Protection Commission, including the correspondence timeline and attached legal analysis.

CLIN-004772  Finally, regarding my Article 15 access request submitted on 7 July 2025, I remind your office that the statutory response deadline is 7 August 2025. This is not a flexible target, nor a matter of internal discretion. It is a binding legal requirement under Article 12(3) GDPR, enforceable by the Data Protection Commission.

CLIN-004773  You noted in your previous message that rectification “is not an absolute right.” Perhaps. But your obligation to respond to a subject access request within one calendar month is absolute. There is no margin for interpretation, no extension by implication, and no substitution of procedure. The response must be full, timely, and accurate—or it is non-compliant.

CLIN-004774  Once again: this is not about you. This is about the ongoing distortion of sensitive data affecting my health and care. The law requires that this data be corrected, and that any process affecting it be timely, accurate, and legally coherent. So far, it has been none of those things.

CLIN-004775  Kind regards,

CLIN-004776  Liam Moloney

CLIN-004844  FINAL GDPR ARTICLE 16 RECTIFICATION REQUEST

CLIN-004845  Subject: Rectification of Structurally Contradictory Medical Record – Patient ID 0360424
CLIN-004846  Date: 15 July 2025
CLIN-004847  From: Liam Moloney
CLIN-004848  To: Data Protection Officer, Galway University Hospital
CLIN-004849  CC: Data Protection Commission, quality and patient safety officials, relevant oversight contacts

CLIN-004850  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-004851  I write under Article 16 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) to submit a final rectification request concerning the total structure, accuracy, and legal standing of my medical record at Galway University Hospital.

CLIN-004852  This request follows two previous Article 16 submissions (dated 12 June and 15 June 2025) and two subsequent reminders (7 July 2025), all of which have gone unanswered in breach of Article 12(3).

CLIN-004853  I. Core Contradiction That Invalidates the Record

CLIN-004854  The medical file assigned to me is structurally and clinically self-nullifying.

CLIN-004855  ● On first presentation in 2017, the treating neurologist issued a written disclaimer stating he was not a movement disorder specialist.
CLIN-004856  ● In 2025, a formal complaint challenging the resulting diagnosis was closed by the same clinician, citing clinical judgment.

CLIN-004857  These two events cannot coexist in a valid or legally compliant record. Either clinical expertise was available (and must be proven), or it was not (and the diagnosis is unsound). There is no defensible middle ground.

CLIN-004858  All content between those bookends — including the diagnosis of idiopathic Parkinson’s disease, its downstream referrals, and governance responses — is founded on a contradiction.

CLIN-004859  This renders the file:

CLIN-004860  ● Materially inaccurate (GDPR Article 5(1)(d))
CLIN-004861  ● Unlawfully unrectified (GDPR Article 16)
CLIN-004862  ● Procedurally mishandled (GDPR Article 12(3))
CLIN-004863  ● Governance-deficient under the Health Act 2004
CLIN-004864  ● Unsuitable for educational reuse under GDPR Article 89

CLIN-004865  II. Diagnostic Accuracy Is Legally Required and Clinically Absent

CLIN-004866  No diagnostic levodopa trial was conducted. The primary symptom that triggered my GP’s referral — bilateral foot pain — was omitted from the clinical narrative. In 2025, I demonstrated dopamine responsiveness of that symptom independently, thereby disproving the peripheral neuropathy assumption that underpinned the Parkinson’s diagnosis.

CLIN-004867  This development:

CLIN-004868  ● Invalidates key assumptions in the diagnosis
CLIN-004869  ● Exposes omission of essential diagnostic steps
CLIN-004870  ● Reinforces that no specialist review was ever obtained

CLIN-004871  The record as it stands is not a diagnostic history; it is a sequence of unsupported conclusions, sustained by silence.

CLIN-004872  III. Institutional Breaches Across Legal, Clinical, and Educational Domains

CLIN-004873  This record — and the hospital’s refusal to correct it — has now implicated no fewer than twelve distinct obligations across law, medicine, and education.

CLIN-004874  ⚖️ Legal Failures:

CLIN-004875  ● GDPR Art. 5(1)(d) – Inaccuracy of special-category health data
CLIN-004876  ● GDPR Art. 16 – Right to rectification
CLIN-004877  ● GDPR Art. 12(3) – Timeliness and acknowledgment
CLIN-004878  ● GDPR Art. 89 – Safeguards for data used in education
CLIN-004879  ● Health Act 2004 – Improper complaint classification (Section 28)
CLIN-004880  ● Data Protection Act 2018 – Failure to remedy material distortion

CLIN-004881  🩺 Clinical Governance Failures:

CLIN-004882  ● Breach of duty to obtain an accurate diagnosis
CLIN-004883  ● Breach of open disclosure policy (HSE)
CLIN-004884  ● Breach of HIQA care standards
CLIN-004885  ● Breach of Medical Council’s ethical record-keeping guidelines

CLIN-004886  🎓 Educational Failures:

CLIN-004887  ● Risk of propagating a distorted case through teaching and training
CLIN-004888  ● Violation of university hospital standards for accurate case modeling

CLIN-004889  This file, if reused for case review, training, or audit, trains clinicians to normalize contradiction, accept inexpert diagnosis as conclusive, and treat silence as governance. That is not education. That is error institutionalized.

CLIN-004890  IV. Formal Rectification Requests

CLIN-004891  You are hereby requested to:

CLIN-004892  1. Fully Rectify and Annotate:

CLIN-004893  ● All diagnostic labels, discharge letters, and summaries referencing idiopathic Parkinson’s disease
CLIN-004894  ● All documents that exclude or fail to examine the original presenting symptom (bilateral foot pain)
CLIN-004895  ● All materials that cite “clinical judgment” from a clinician outside scope

CLIN-004896  2. Withdraw or Restrict:

CLIN-004897  ● The use of my record in educational, training, or governance contexts
CLIN-004898  ● Any unannotated circulation of my file in internal reviews or learning systems

CLIN-004899  3. Declare and Document:

CLIN-004900  ● All governance decisions regarding my rectification requests
CLIN-004901  ● Any teaching use of my record in internal or external contexts since 2017

CLIN-004902  V. Regulatory Escalation

CLIN-004903  This submission is copied to the Data Protection Commission and relevant HSE governance contacts.

CLIN-004904  If no response is issued within the extended statutory period, a compliance order and supervisory investigation will be formally pursued, and all supporting evidence submitted in full.

CLIN-004905  VI. Closing

CLIN-004906  This is not a case of misunderstanding. It is the end result of diagnostic abandonment, compounded by eight years of institutional non-correction.

CLIN-004907  You did not inherit a broken file. You built it — entry by entry, and omission by omission — atop a known and declared contradiction.

CLIN-004908  I am not demanding anything more than the law, medicine, and ethics already require:

CLIN-004909  That the diagnosis be accurate. That the record be honest. That the institution act.

CLIN-004910  Sincerely,

CLIN-004911  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005007  **Forensic status:** Primary contemporaneous evidence connecting the patient’s historical reconstruction to an immediate request for care, and the GP’s express scope limitation.

CLIN-005009  ## Patient

CLIN-005010  Dear Dr Brennan,

CLIN-005011  I am writing to you regarding my ongoing management while awaiting my dystonia clinic appointment at St Vincent’s in November 2025.

CLIN-005012  Your referral letter of 29 June 2017 described my presentation at that time: an unusual stiff gait, bilateral foot problems, clawing of the toes in bed, and difficulty relaxing because of muscle tension. Eight years later, foot pain remains the central issue. Despite different diagnoses and treatments, the problem you first recorded has never been resolved.

CLIN-005013  After 2017, my management was framed through idiopathic Parkinson’s disease. The foot pain you identified was not included under that label. Yet in March 2025, Sinemet CR given overnight demonstrated that the pain is dopaminergic-responsive: it reduced pain, reduced suicidality, and restored clarity. This shows that the pain is not peripheral or incidental, but central.

CLIN-005014  I also want to acknowledge that procyclidine, though later withdrawn due to raised intraocular pressure and glaucoma risk, produced a striking anticholinergic response. At the time, this was not carried forward diagnostically. Taken together, my history demonstrates both dopaminergic and anticholinergic responsiveness.

CLIN-005015  My current difficulty is this: I am carefully using Sinemet CR and IR, but the IR in particular causes painful dyskinesia at standard doses. Even at half doses, relief is barely balanced by harm. I am therefore asking you to consider what can be done in the interim to support me until November, given that the original problem you identified — painful dystonic drive from the feet — has persisted to this day.

CLIN-005016  I understand the hierarchy of care and that your hands are often tied by neurology’s direction. But the logic is clear: if foot pain was central in 2017, and is still central in 2025, then both of us have been constrained by an institutional omission. I would be grateful if you could consider what support you can provide now, within your remit, to bridge this gap.

CLIN-005017  Yours sincerely,
CLIN-005018  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005019  ## GP response

CLIN-005020  > Hi Liam,
CLIN-005021  >
CLIN-005022  > I am certainly minded to help but as a GP I cannot really be adjusting your levodopa meds without Neurology specialist guidance as that would be well beyond my ordinary scope of expertise. If your appointment is not until November, have you any possible input from the Parkinsons Specialist Nurse or anything before then?
CLIN-005023  >
CLIN-005024  > Regards,
CLIN-005025  >
CLIN-005026  > Dr Aaron Brennan

CLIN-005073  DATE: 6 September 2025
CLIN-005074  GDPR RIGHT / TYPE: GUH decision on the 12 June 2025 Article 16 clinical-data request
CLIN-005075  SENDER: Nuala Madden, SARS Co-ordinator, University Hospital Galway / Galway University Hospitals
CLIN-005076  RECIPIENT: Mr Liam Moloney
CLIN-005077  CC/BCC: NOT SHOWN IN THE PRIMARY LETTER
CLIN-005078  SUBJECT / REFERENCE: Request for rectification of medical records.
CLIN-005079  TIMESTAMP: NOT AVAILABLE IN THE PRIMARY LETTER. Letter is dated 6 September 2025.
CLIN-005080  SOURCE: Primary decision letter preserved as “Sept 6th Refusal.pdf”.

CLIN-005081  COMPLETE SUBSTANTIVE RESPONSE

CLIN-005082  Dear Mr Moloney,

CLIN-005083  I refer to your request made on 12th June, 2025 for the erasure of a record, held in your medical file at Galway University Hospital. Firstly, I wish to apologise for the delay in issuing you a decision.

CLIN-005084  Your request in summary refers to the following:

CLIN-005085  1. “Rectify all relevant documents where the absence of my core symptoms (severe, disabling foot pain) misrepresents my diagnostic journey.”

CLIN-005086  2. “Annotate all files (referrals, discharge summaries, diagnostic letters) that present a conclusive Parkinson’s diagnosis, noting the lack of dopaminergic diagnostic trial and subsequent evidence in 2025.”

CLIN-005087  The decision has been made that the personal information contained in your medical records to which your application refers is not judged to be misleading for the following reasons.

CLIN-005088  The HSE relies on Articles 6 and 9 of the GDPR as the legal basis for processing a patient’s personal and medical data. Article 6(1)(e) of GDPR is relied upon for processing the individual’s personal data which states that processing shall be lawful if “processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest or in the exercise of official authority vested in the controller”. For the HSE this lawful authority is vested in us through the Health Act 2004.

CLIN-005089  Sections 36(1) & 52(1) of the Act cover suitable and specific measures of processing and of special categories of personal data for the provision of healthcare. I am satisfied that the HSE has met its legal requirements under the Act and is in compliance with GDPR.

CLIN-005090  A diagnosis in a medical file is the reflection of the professional opinion of the doctor at that moment of time and based on the information available to that moment in time. On this basis the HSE has a lawful basis for continued processing of your medical records in accordance with Article 7(1) of GDPR.

CLIN-005091  As a consequence, it remains factually accurate to record the opinion or diagnosis that was made and what the opinion or diagnosis was, such data would not be “inaccurate” as it is a factually accurate reflection that an opinion or diagnosis occurred and what the opinion or diagnosis was at that point in time and is still necessary in relation to the purposes for which the personal data was originally collected or otherwise processed and is consistent with Article 17(3)(c) in providing comprehensive medical assessment and treatment of the individual.

CLIN-005092  In addition, a medical opinion or diagnosis provides important context for any subsequent medical treatment decisions made by Health Care providers. A prior opinion or diagnosis may explain why certain medications were prescribed or certain treatment plans were undertaken.

CLIN-005093  Maintaining the medical record in its current form, serves the public interest by ensuring the service user and the treating Healthcare Professionals have a reliable record of what has taken place.

CLIN-005094  Following consideration of the provisions of the above legislation, it has been decided to refuse your request, as there is an obligation on the HSE and its staff to keep accurate records which are essential for the continuity of care and comprehensive enough to allow Health Practitioners to be fully informed of factors which impact on the provision of care to be provided.

CLIN-005095  Rights to appeal

CLIN-005096  If you are unhappy with this decision you may make a complaint in writing to the Office of the Data Protection Commission, 21 Fitzwilliam Square South, Dublin 2, D02R2B8. This will involve an independent investigation of the matter by the Data Protection Commission.

CLIN-005097  Yours sincerely,

CLIN-005098  (signed)

CLIN-005099  Nuala Madden

CLIN-005100  SARS Co-ordinator,
CLIN-005101  University Hospital Galway.

CLIN-005102  ---

CLIN-005109  DATE: 6 September 2025
CLIN-005110  GDPR RIGHT / TYPE: GUH decision on the 15 June 2025 Article 16 QPS closure-letter request
CLIN-005111  SENDER: Nuala Madden, SARS Coordinator, Galway University Hospitals
CLIN-005112  RECIPIENT: Mr Liam Moloney
CLIN-005113  CC/BCC: NOT SHOWN IN THE PRIMARY LETTER
CLIN-005114  SUBJECT / REFERENCE: Request for rectification of medical records.
CLIN-005115  TIMESTAMP: NOT AVAILABLE IN THE PRIMARY LETTER. Letter is dated 6 September 2025.
CLIN-005116  SOURCE: Primary decision letter preserved as “Sept 6th refusal II.pdf”.

CLIN-005117  COMPLETE SUBSTANTIVE RESPONSE

CLIN-005118  Dear Mr. Moloney,

CLIN-005119  I refer to your request dated 15th June, 2025 made under Article 16 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) for the:

CLIN-005120  “Immediate rectification of all Quality and Patient Safety (QPS) ‘closure letters’ issued by Galway University Hospital that

CLIN-005121  ● Invoke the Health Act 2004 as justification for rerouting complaints back to the original clinician; and

CLIN-005122  ● Use the term ‘assessment’ to describe an undocumented non-transparent internal process that classifies complaints as ‘solely clinical’ without independent review or appeal.”

CLIN-005123  I wish to advise that the GDPR does provide individuals with the right to rectification of inaccurate personal data and the right to erasure in specific circumstances. However, your request does not seem to relate to the rectification of your personal data.

CLIN-005124  Personal data as defined under the GDPR means any information relating to an identified or identifiable natural person (‘data subject’); therefore a Quality and Patient Safety “closure letters” issued by Galway University Hospital does not relate to you as a data subject.

CLIN-005125  Therefore, my decision is to refuse this request for the amendment of records, as it does not relate to your personal data and does not come within the scope of personal data as defined under the GDPR.

CLIN-005126  If you are unhappy with this decision, you may make a complaint in writing to the Office of the Data Protection Commissioner, 21 Fitzwilliam Square South, Dublin 2, D02RD28.

CLIN-005127  Yours sincerely,

CLIN-005128  (signed)

CLIN-005129  Nuala Madden

CLIN-005130  SARS Coordinator,
CLIN-005131  Galway University Hospitals.

CLIN-005132  ---

CLIN-005178  24 NOVEMBER 2025

CLIN-005179  EMAIL

CLIN-005180  From: Liam Moloney <[patient email redacted]>
CLIN-005181  To: Professor David J. Burn, President <president@university.ie>
CLIN-005182  Cc: Data Protection Officer, University of Galway <dpo@university.ie>; Professor Michal Molcho, Head of School of Education <dean.education@university.ie>; Professor Rosemary Geoghegan, Head of Discipline of Medical Education (Clinical Education Lead) <director.clinicaleducation@university.ie>
CLIN-005183  Subject: Safeguarding, Academic Oversight, and Enclosed GDPR Access Request Concerning a 2017 University-Affiliated Teaching Interaction
CLIN-005184  Date: 24 November 2025

CLIN-005185  Dear President Burn,

CLIN-005186  I hope this message finds you well.

CLIN-005187  I am writing to you directly because the matter enclosed intersects not only with clinical governance within Galway University Hospitals, but also with the academic, supervisory, and educational responsibilities of the University’s School of Medicine. Given your recent appointment and your own expertise in movement disorders, I felt it appropriate to bring this matter to your attention with the full respect owed to your office.

CLIN-005188  In July 2017, during my inpatient admission to St. Anthony’s Ward, I was the subject of a bedside teaching interaction involving four trainee doctors under University supervision. This interaction is now of acute relevance not only from a clinical perspective, but from an academic and governance perspective. My personal data, my presentation, and my clinical concerns were processed in a formal teaching setting. That makes the University a data controller for the purposes of GDPR in respect of material arising from that interaction.

CLIN-005189  The attached GDPR request explains the broader context: a multi-year diagnostic failure, the withholding of levodopa for eight and a half years, and the subsequent discovery in 2025—by myself—that levodopa resolved my symptoms immediately. The context is deeply concerning. But what brings the University into the matter is that the 2017 teaching event modelled an unaddressed clinical concern as if it were resolved. The students witnessed my bilateral foot pain being dismissed rather than investigated, and the interaction occurred under University governance.

CLIN-005190  As a former student of this University, and as a citizen who still lives in the city we share, I felt it important that you be aware that this teaching moment has now become central to understanding an ongoing safeguarding risk. I am including the attached GDPR request not as a complaint against the University, but as a necessary attempt to ensure teaching data, supervision records, and academic material held by the University are accurate, lawful, and safe.

CLIN-005191  I also wish to add, with complete respect for your time and position, that my clinical reality today is very simple: I need an anticholinergic. Any consultant can verify this in minutes by checking the pharmacology, my levodopa response, and my recent functional changes. You may also, if you choose, examine the history yourself — the urgent referral that went unacted upon, the DAT-scan referral whose wording defies clinical reasoning, and the fact that the so-called “second opinion” I was given was not from a movement-disorder specialist at all, but from a geneticist. These facts matter because since the day of that 2017 teaching encounter, I have needed an anticholinergic, and I did not have one.

CLIN-005192  Before closing, I want to speak plainly on one human point that underlies everything I have written. For eight and a half years I have lived with preventable, dopamine-responsive suffering — physical misery that should never have been allowed to take root. My request to the University is made in good faith, but beneath the governance and GDPR dimensions lies a simple appeal for relief: I need an anticholinergic. It is the one treatment that offers me immediate functional stability, and I have gone without it because my pathway has been blocked. If anyone in a position of authority can help ensure that the structures preventing access to basic symptomatic care are moved aside, it would bring an end to misery that has gone on far too long.

CLIN-005193  Thank you for taking the time to consider this.

CLIN-005194  Yours sincerely,
CLIN-005195  Liam Moloney
CLIN-005196  [home address redacted]
CLIN-005197  Email: [patient email redacted]
CLIN-005198  Phone: [patient phone redacted]


CLIN-005199  ATTACHMENT

CLIN-005200  Article 15 / 16 / 18 GDPR Request to the University Data Protection Officer

CLIN-005201  To: University Data Protection Officer
CLIN-005202  Cc: President Burn; Dean of Medicine; Director of Clinical Education
CLIN-005203  Subject: GDPR Access, Rectification, and Restriction Request Concerning University-Linked Teaching Interaction (10 July 2017 (morning bedside teaching session for a new cohort of trainees))

CLIN-005204  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-005205  Under Articles 15, 16, and 18 of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), I am requesting access to, rectification of, and where necessary restriction of processing relating to any University-held personal data arising from my involvement in a bedside teaching interaction during my inpatient admission to St. Anthony’s Ward, Galway University Hospital, on or around 10–11 July 2017.

CLIN-005206  During this interaction, which was conducted under University teaching governance:
CLIN-005207  • Four trainee doctors participated under a supervised educational structure;
CLIN-005208  • I raised a serious clinical concern (disabling bilateral foot pain);
CLIN-005209  • I requested senior neurological review;
CLIN-005210  • A dismissive response was modelled to the students; and
CLIN-005211  • The interaction took place on 10 July 2017 in the morning, during the arrival of a new cohort of trainees, making it particularly memorable for those present.

CLIN-005212  This interaction constituted a teaching moment with clear academic, supervisory, and governance implications.

CLIN-005213  Given the University’s role in clinical education, it is likely that my personal data—including observations, notes, trainee reflections, logbooks, supervisory materials, and related teaching documentation—was processed by the University or its agents for educational or academic purposes.

CLIN-005214  Accordingly, I request:

CLIN-005215  Article 15 – Access

CLIN-005216  All University-held personal data relating to:
CLIN-005217  • The 10–11 July 2017 teaching interaction;
CLIN-005218  • Any trainee records, logbooks, case-based discussions, or notes referencing my presentation;
CLIN-005219  • Any material created by lecturers or supervisors referencing my case in teaching contexts;
CLIN-005220  • Any academic governance documentation relating to bedside teaching on St. Anthony’s Ward during this period.

CLIN-005221  If third-party data is present, I request redaction rather than refusal, in accordance with Article 15(4).

CLIN-005222  Article 16 – Rectification

CLIN-005223  If any University-held teaching records, supervisory materials, or academic documents contain inaccurate or incomplete information about my condition, clinical presentation, or treatment pathway—including information derived from later inaccurate hospital records—I request rectification.

CLIN-005224  This includes, but is not limited to, any teaching material implying that my concern had been addressed, evaluated, escalated, or resolved, when it was not.

CLIN-005225  Article 18 – Restriction of Processing

CLIN-005226  Until all data accuracy issues are resolved, I request restriction on any further teaching, presentation, analysis, or academic use of my case.

CLIN-005227  Basis for This Request

CLIN-005228  My case is clinically and academically unusual, and this forms the foundation for involving the University as a data controller. The facts are these:
CLIN-005229  • At age 39, I was labelled with young-onset Parkinson’s disease without a levodopa trial being performed — a deviation from standard diagnostic protocol.
CLIN-005230  • My “second opinion” was not provided by a movement-disorder specialist, but by a geneticist, which is atypical and clinically incongruous for a Parkinsonian diagnosis.
CLIN-005231  • The diagnosing consultant explicitly disclaimed expertise in my discharge summary, making the absence of a levodopa trial even more significant.
CLIN-005232  • My Parkinsonism was backdated from age 39 to an alleged onset at age 26, which is exceptionally rare and academically noteworthy.
CLIN-005233  • Patients in my position — individuals supposedly symptomatic for over a decade without a levodopa trial — are nearly unheard of in modern neurology. Cases like this are usually escalated, discussed, written up, or used as pivotal teaching material.
CLIN-005234  • If my case has appeared in University teaching materials, those records must be accurate and GDPR-compliant. If it has not appeared, that itself signals an academic governance problem, as such a rare profile would normally warrant review.

CLIN-005235  These facts create legitimate grounds for access, rectification, and restriction. They raise important questions for the University regarding accuracy of teaching content, supervision of clinical education, and whether students were exposed to an atypical or misleading depiction of neurological assessment.

CLIN-005236  I request confirmation of receipt of this request and a full response within the statutory one-month timeframe.

CLIN-005237  Sincerely,
CLIN-005238  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005323  This complaint arises from the University’s complete non-engagement with a GDPR request served on 24 November 2025, the expiry of the statutory response period under Article 12(3) on 24 December 2025, and continued silence thereafter, including failure to confirm restriction of processing under Article 18 or to state whether relevant personal data exists or has been processed.
