CLIN-005333  ATTACHMENT

CLIN-005334  Failure by University Controller to Comply with Articles 12, 15, 16, 18 and 5(1)(d) GDPR

CLIN-005335  1. Data Subject

CLIN-005336  Name: Liam Moloney
CLIN-005337  Address: [home address redacted]
CLIN-005338  Email: [patient email redacted]

CLIN-005339  2. Controller Complained Of

CLIN-005340  University of Galway

CLIN-005341  In its capacity as an independent data controller for University-governed teaching, supervision, and academic processing.

CLIN-005342  3. Nature of the Complaint

CLIN-005343  I submit this complaint under Article 77 GDPR concerning the University’s failure to comply with its obligations under Articles 12, 15, 16, 18, and 5(1)(d) GDPR in respect of personal data arising from a University-affiliated ward-based teaching interaction in 2017.

CLIN-005344  The complaint concerns complete non-engagement by the University with a valid GDPR request, failure to respond within the statutory timeframe, failure to confirm restriction of processing, and failure to clarify whether relevant personal data exists or has been processed, notwithstanding formal notice of expiry and subsequent developments materially affecting data accuracy.

CLIN-005345  4. Relevant Chronology

CLIN-005346  24 November 2025

CLIN-005347  I submitted a written GDPR request to the University engaging Articles 12, 15, 16, and 18 GDPR, concerning the accuracy and processing of personal data arising from a University-governed teaching interaction in 2017. Senior University officers were copied.

CLIN-005348  19 December 2025

CLIN-005349  A definitive specialist diagnosis of dystonia was made in my case by Dr Seán Riordan, Consultant Neurologist at St Vincent’s Hospital, Dublin. This diagnosis materially reframes the clinical assumptions under which the 2017 teaching interaction occurred.

CLIN-005350  24 December 2025

CLIN-005351  The Article 12(3) one-month statutory response period expired. No response, refusal, extension notice, or confirmation of restriction was received from the University.

CLIN-005352  On the same date, I issued a written Notice of Statutory Expiry and Record Preservation to the University, explicitly recording:
CLIN-005353  • expiry of the statutory response period;
CLIN-005354  • absence of any substantive response;
CLIN-005355  • absence of any notification of extension under Article 12(3);
CLIN-005356  • absence of any confirmation of restriction of processing under Article 18; and
CLIN-005357  • absence of any clarification as to whether the University holds, should hold, or has processed relevant personal data.

CLIN-005358  That notice also recorded the 19 December 2025 dystonia diagnosis and its relevance to the accuracy and contextual integrity of any teaching-related personal data.

CLIN-005359  Post-expiry

CLIN-005360  As of the date of this complaint, the University has issued no response, no refusal, no clarification, and no confirmation of restriction, and has not engaged substantively despite notice of expiry and express opportunity to respond out of time.

CLIN-005361  5. GDPR Obligations Engaged

CLIN-005362  Article 12(3) — Time Limits

CLIN-005363  The University failed to respond within one month and failed to notify any lawful extension.

CLIN-005364  Article 15 — Right of Access

CLIN-005365  The University failed to state whether it holds, should hold, or has processed personal data relating to the teaching interaction.

CLIN-005366  Article 16 — Right to Rectification

CLIN-005367  The University failed to engage with a notified accuracy dispute concerning the assumptions underlying teaching-related processing.

CLIN-005368  Article 18 — Restriction of Processing

CLIN-005369  Despite an explicit request and an ongoing accuracy dispute involving special-category health data, the University failed to confirm or implement restriction of processing.

CLIN-005370  Article 5(1)(d) — Accuracy

CLIN-005371  Following notification of a definitive specialist diagnosis materially reframing the underlying assumptions, the University’s continued silence and continued processing posture constitute failure to ensure accuracy and contextual integrity of personal data.

CLIN-005372  6. Nature of the Non-Compliance

CLIN-005373  This complaint does not concern a minor delay or administrative oversight.

CLIN-005374  It concerns:
CLIN-005375  • complete failure to engage with a valid GDPR request;
CLIN-005376  • continued silence after statutory expiry was formally notified; and
CLIN-005377  • continued processing posture in the face of confirmed correction of the disputed assumption.

CLIN-005378  At no point did the University:
CLIN-005379  • deny that processing occurred;
CLIN-005380  • confirm that no data exists;
CLIN-005381  • restrict processing;
CLIN-005382  • or explain its position.

CLIN-005383  7. Outcome Sought

CLIN-005384  I respectfully request that the Commission:

CLIN-005385  1. Investigate the University’s compliance with Articles 12, 15, 16, 18, and 5(1)(d) GDPR;
CLIN-005386  2. Determine whether the University has unlawfully failed to restrict processing of disputed special-category personal data;
CLIN-005387  3. Require the University to state clearly whether relevant personal data exists or has been processed;
CLIN-005388  4. Require appropriate rectification, annotation, or restriction of any such data; and
CLIN-005389  5. Consider whether corrective or enforcement measures are warranted in light of continued non-compliance after notice.

CLIN-005390  8. Closing

CLIN-005391  This complaint is confined strictly to data-protection compliance.

CLIN-005392  It does not seek adjudication of clinical matters.

CLIN-005393  The record demonstrates lawful restraint, clear notice, expiry, and continued silence. I submit that the matter is now properly before the Commission.

CLIN-005394  Signed:
CLIN-005395  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005414  I refer to previous correspondence in relation to the complaint submitted by you to the Data Protection Commission (“the DPC”) against University Hospital Galway.

CLIN-005415  As previously advised by my colleague, your complaint has been transferred to the Complaint Assessment and Early Resolution Unit. The DPC is in receipt of a large volume of complaints, which all require adequate time and attention. There are a number of complaints which were received by the DPC prior to your complaint.

CLIN-005416  I have included a link to our website which sets out our complaint handling process: https://www.dataprotection.ie/en/individuals/exercising-your-rights/complaints-handling-investigations-and-enforcement-individuals

CLIN-005417  As you can see, one of the first stages is ‘Assessment’. The case officer may need further information from you so that they can properly assess your complaint. I note that your complaint relates to requests made by you to exercise your rights, and concerns regarding the processing of personal data.

CLIN-005418  Information that the case officer will typically request is:

CLIN-005419  Request exercising rights

CLIN-005420  · copy of the request

CLIN-005421  · reminder letter sent to the data controller

CLIN-005422  · any other relevant correspondence on the matter, for example, if the data controller has sought proof of your identity, evidence that you have provided same

CLIN-005423  Processing concerns
CLIN-005424  · details of the specific data protection issue you are raising

CLIN-005425  · documents that support the allegation being made

CLIN-005426  · copy of relevant correspondence exchanged with the data controller on the matter

CLIN-005427  I would suggest that you review the information you have submitted with your complaint, and provide anything you think you may have omitted. This will save time once a case officer has been assigned to your complaint.

CLIN-005428  If you have not already done so, you may wish to consider contacting University Hospital Galway to let them know that you have submitted a complaint to the DPC. It may prompt them to resolve the matter you are complaining about, and result in a speedier resolution for you.

CLIN-005429  If you have any queries about your complaint, or you want to submit further information about your complaint, don’t hesitate to contact me at this email address.

CLIN-005438  **Date / period:** 14 January 2026
CLIN-005439  **Voice / origin:** Patient to GUH/HSE DPO

CLIN-005443  DATE: 14 January 2026
CLIN-005444  GDPR RIGHT / TYPE: Renewed Article 16 rectification request read together with Articles 5(1)(d), 12, 18, 19 and 24; Article 18 restriction expressly asserted
CLIN-005445  SENDER: Liam Moloney <[patient email redacted]>
CLIN-005446  RECIPIENT: dpo@guh.hse.ie
CLIN-005447  CC: None shown in primary Gmail record
CLIN-005448  BCC: None shown in primary Gmail record
CLIN-005449  SUBJECT: Subject: GDPR Article 16 Rectification & Article 18 Restriction — Re-engagement Following DPC Correspondence (Ref: C-25-7-2074)
CLIN-005450  TIMESTAMP: 2026-01-14 00:06:02 UTC.
CLIN-005451  ATTACHMENTS: None.

CLIN-005452  COMPLETE SUBSTANTIVE TEXT

CLIN-005453  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-005454  I write in my capacity as data subject in connection with an open complaint before the Data Protection Commission (Ref: C-25-7-2074) concerning unresolved GDPR accuracy, rectification, and restriction failures by Galway University Hospitals.

CLIN-005455  Following recent correspondence from the Data Protection Commission, I am re-engaging with the controller to provide a further opportunity to reconcile these matters in accordance with the GDPR framework.

CLIN-005456  This correspondence therefore constitutes a renewed request under Article 16 GDPR, read together with Articles 5(1)(d), 12, 18, 19, and 24, and engages the controller’s statutory obligations and timelines under Article 12(3).

CLIN-005457  This re-engagement is undertaken in light of a material change affecting data accuracy and the Data Protection Commission’s indication that the controller now has an opportunity to reconcile these matters in accordance with GDPR.

CLIN-005458  Material Change Affecting Accuracy

CLIN-005459  On 19 December 2025, I received an authoritative diagnosis of dystonia, explicitly not Parkinson’s disease.

CLIN-005460  For GDPR purposes, this diagnosis is material because it explains the original 2017 GP referral and confirms that the governing presenting features were omitted from the discharge summary and downstream records.

CLIN-005461  As a result, personal data that was previously treated as disputed is now demonstrably incomplete and misleading as a current data state, engaging the controller’s continuous accuracy obligation under Article 5(1)(d).

CLIN-005462  Recharacterisation of Prior Accuracy Disputes

CLIN-005463  Article 16 rectification requests raised in June–July 2025 were treated as casting doubt upon settled findings.

CLIN-005464  In light of the dystonia diagnosis, those requests are properly understood as identifying unresolved defects of accuracy and completeness, not matters of disagreement.

CLIN-005465  The material fact now known removes the basis on which rectification was previously avoided and converts those disputes into determinative accuracy issues requiring reconciliation.

CLIN-005466  Administrative Finality on Incomplete Data

CLIN-005467  The issue now requiring reconciliation is not the exercise of clinical judgment.

CLIN-005468  The issue is the achievement of administrative finality on an incomplete representation of how the data subject presented at referral and admission.

CLIN-005469  The GDPR exists to prevent precisely this outcome, where omission and premature conclusiveness combine to render personal data misleading despite historical provenance.

CLIN-005470  Prolonged Reliance and Material Consequence

CLIN-005471  The accuracy failures identified were not abstract or theoretical.

CLIN-005472  The continued reliance on disputed and incomplete data produced prolonged and preventable consequences, including sustained mischaracterisation of my clinical profile and foreclosure of appropriate diagnostic consideration.

CLIN-005473  This establishes that the data at issue was operationally relied upon, engaging Articles 5(1)(d) and 24 in a material sense.

CLIN-005474  Article 18 Restriction and SIMT Processing

CLIN-005475  On 4 August 2025, I lawfully invoked Article 18 GDPR due to unresolved accuracy disputes.

CLIN-005476  Notwithstanding this restriction, the controller proceeded with SIMT, relied upon the disputed data set, and issued a substantive outcome stating that there was “no deficit in care.”

CLIN-005477  This sequence constitutes a compound GDPR failure.

CLIN-005478  Restricted data was actively relied upon in breach of Article 18.

CLIN-005479  Definitive downstream personal data was generated from a contested accuracy state in breach of Article 5(1)(d).

CLIN-005480  No notification or qualification was provided to recipients of that outcome in breach of Article 19.

CLIN-005481  Organisational measures failed to give effect to restriction in breach of Article 24.

CLIN-005482  These positions cannot coexist without corrective action.

CLIN-005483  Rectification and Reconciliation Required

CLIN-005484  I therefore require the controller to reconcile, by rectification or lawful refusal, the following non-exhaustive accuracy failures:

CLIN-005485  The continued unqualified representation of Parkinson’s disease as a current diagnosis.

CLIN-005486  The omission of the primary presenting symptom and dystonia-consistent gait features from operative summaries.

CLIN-005487  The representation of diagnostic sequence without qualification where the principal discriminating intervention was identified but not performed.

CLIN-005488  The failure to integrate or annotate contradictory pharmacological evidence subsequently acknowledged.

CLIN-005489  The reliance on disputed data to generate SIMT findings while an Article 18 restriction was in force.

CLIN-005490  The mischaracterisation of prior GDPR rectification requests within administrative and governance records.

CLIN-005491  Rectification may take the form of annotation, qualification, or completion, provided the outcome satisfies Article 5(1)(d).

CLIN-005492  Continuing Effect of Restriction

CLIN-005493  The Article 18 restriction remains in force pending reconciliation.

CLIN-005494  Any continued reliance or operational use of the disputed data constitutes a fresh GDPR breach.

CLIN-005495  Article 19 Notification

CLIN-005496  Where rectification or restriction is effected, the controller is reminded of its obligation under Article 19 to notify all recipients of the personal data, including any downstream determinations derived from restricted inputs.

CLIN-005497  Statutory Timelines

CLIN-005498  This renewed rectification request engages the controller’s obligations under Article 12(3).

CLIN-005499  A written response is required within one month of receipt.

CLIN-005500  All correspondence should be conducted in writing for traceability.

CLIN-005501  Purpose of This Engagement

CLIN-005502  This correspondence is issued as a prospective GDPR compliance engagement following supervisory contact.

CLIN-005503  It is not a complaint, appeal, or clinical request.

CLIN-005504  It is an opportunity for the controller to reconcile personal data accuracy and restricted processing in accordance with GDPR.

CLIN-005505  Yours sincerely,

CLIN-005506  Liam Moloney
CLIN-005507  Data Subject
CLIN-005508  Patient ID: 0360424
CLIN-005509  Galway, Ireland

CLIN-005526  COMPLETE SUBSTANTIVE RESPONSE

CLIN-005527  Dear Mr. Moloney,

CLIN-005528  Thank you for your correspondence.

CLIN-005529  The Quality & Patient Safety Department has considered the governance issues raised. The complaint assessment and subsequent SIMT review were undertaken using the information, clinical presentations, and investigations available at the time, and the determinations reached were appropriate, specific to a predefined timeframe, and compliant with established governance frameworks. A subsequent diagnosis of dystonia in December 2025 does not undermine the validity of those processes or outcomes. The SIMT assesses the quality and appropriateness of care based on contemporaneous data.

CLIN-005530  In that regard, the updated diagnosis does not necessitate reopening governance reviews, altering prior determinations, or retrospectively amending historical medical records. Updated diagnoses supersede earlier working diagnoses clinically, but do not render prior governance processes unlawful or unsafe. On this basis, QPS does not identify a current governance risk arising from continued reliance on the completed processes, nor is escalation to another governance function indicated.

CLIN-005531  Thank you for bringing these matters to our attention and for the opportunity to respond.

CLIN-005532  Kind regards,

CLIN-005533  Emily Comerford

CLIN-005534  On Behalf of the Quality and Patient Safety Team

CLIN-005705  The decision letter states that the patient’s Article 15 request dated 15 July 2026 was “received in this office on 14th August 2026.” It reproduces the requested Podiatry categories, including referrals/referral-processing records; clinical notes and treatment; appointments/waiting lists; discharge/closure/removal records; communications with Neurology, the GP or other HSE services; and photographs, diagrams or test results.

CLIN-005708  The second page reproduces the additional Article 15 information sought and states in substance that the personal data was stored in line with the HSE retention policy 2025–2028 and “has not been processed in any other way, other than detailed in the records provided.”

CLIN-005710  The decision gives the Data Protection Commission complaint route and is signed by Siobhán Curran, Decision Maker.

CLIN-005711  The associated 27-page Podiatry disclosure itself is already transcribed in the pre-existing master at DOCUMENT 19 (CLIN-002818 onward).

CLIN-005713  # DOCUMENT 70 — Formal Complaint Regarding Incomplete Article 15 Response — SAR 2026-983

CLIN-005714  **Date / period:** 17 August 2026
CLIN-005715  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient complaint to DDPO West
CLIN-005716  **Provenance:** Connected Gmail message 1a010b88fc492e7e

CLIN-005718  **Source-status note:** This is a patient complaint about completeness and timing, not a finding that HSE breached Article 15.

CLIN-005719  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-005720  I refer to my Article 15 GDPR Subject Access Request dated 15 July 2026 concerning my Podiatry/Chiropody personal data at Merlin Park University Hospital, and to the records and decision correspondence supplied to me today, 17 August 2026.

CLIN-005721  I am grateful that the Podiatry records located by the responding unit have now been released. I am, however, making a formal data-protection complaint to the HSE because the response does not appear to satisfy the Article 15 request that I actually submitted.

CLIN-005722  The chronology is important.

CLIN-005723  My request was submitted to DDPO West on 15 July 2026. DDPO West acknowledged receipt on 20 July and requested identification, which I supplied the same day. On 23 July I was informed under reference SAR 2026-983 that the request had been forwarded for processing.

CLIN-005724  The decision sent today is from Siobhán Curran, Freedom of Information & Subject Access Request Manager. Her letter states that my request dated 15 July was “received in this office on 14th August 2026” and states: “I have made the decision to fully release your Podiatry records.”

CLIN-005725  I accept that the records held by that particular Podiatry/FOI-SAR unit may have been fully released. My concern is that full release of those records does not, by itself, constitute a complete response to the Article 15 request submitted on 15 July.

CLIN-005726  [The email then enumerates the requested categories and Article 15 information, asks what systems/locations were searched, whether each requested category was searched, whether no-record results can be confirmed, whether responsive material exists elsewhere, and whether recipients/source/other Article 15 information remains outstanding.]

CLIN-005727  For clarity, I am not complaining that the Podiatry records supplied today should not have been released. I am grateful for their release. My complaint is that the statement that my Podiatry records have been “fully” released does not answer whether my Article 15 request as a whole has been complied with.

CLIN-005728  Kind regards,
CLIN-005729  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005733  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient complaint to Data Protection Commission
CLIN-005734  **Provenance:** Connected Gmail message 1a02510b878ee4ee

CLIN-005736  **Source-status note:** This is a complaint/allegation to the regulator. It is not a DPC determination. The DPC acknowledgment and later handling should be added when substantive case correspondence is received.

CLIN-005737  Dear Data Protection Commission,

CLIN-005738  I wish to make a formal complaint under Article 77 GDPR concerning HSE SAR 2026-983.

CLIN-005739  This was a single Article 15 access request submitted to HSE on 15 July 2026. As of 21 August 2026, it remains unfinished.

CLIN-005740  On 17 August, Siobhán Curran issued a decision stating: “I have made the decision to fully release your Podiatry records.”

CLIN-005741  I received records, but the response did not answer the full Article 15 request I had submitted. I therefore complained to DDPO West that evening and asked the Data Protection Officer to establish whether the complete request had actually been searched and answered.

CLIN-005742  Today, 21 August, I received two HSE emails within approximately an hour.

CLIN-005743  First, Siobhán Curran informed me that my complaint had reached her office, that she is now on planned leave until 31 August, that there will be no staff in the Roscommon FOI office during that period, and that she will “endeavour to address” my email when she returns.

CLIN-005744  Shortly afterwards, DDPO West confirmed that it had referred my complaint back to Siobhán Curran, describing her as the “SAR Decision Maker, HSE West Primary Care Services.”

CLIN-005745  If Siobhán Curran had already provided the full response to my Article 15 request, it is unclear what remains for her to “endeavour” to address after 31 August. If further work is required to answer my complaint about missing searches, records or Article 15 information, then the access request was not complete when HSE purported to conclude it on 17 August.

CLIN-005746  I therefore ask the Commission to examine whether HSE has complied with Articles 12 and 15 GDPR, in particular whether my request was answered completely, the complete organisational scope was searched, all required Article 15 information was supplied, and the applicable timeframe was complied with.

CLIN-005748  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005752  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient correspondence to GUH/HSE DPO; DPC copied

The source is included to establish what the controller and regulator were told and when; it is not a DPC finding. The source itself says “15 July” for the final Article 16 request, whereas other preserved material dates the consolidated clarification 16 July; that source-level discrepancy is intentionally not silently reconciled.

CLIN-005756  Dear Data Protection Officer,

CLIN-005757  This letter constitutes a formal and final notification that Galway University Hospital is in breach of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) on multiple grounds, across multiple submissions, and over a sustained period.

CLIN-005758  This communication now serves as:

CLIN-005759  - A cover letter to the institutional record;
CLIN-005760  - A 7-day final reminder before enforcement escalation;
CLIN-005761  - And a complete consolidation of all known unresolved data breaches and failures of statutory compliance under GDPR.

CLIN-005762  Status of Requests and Correspondence

CLIN-005763  1. Three Article 16 Rectification Requests (Now Closed and Breached)

CLIN-005764  - 12 June 2025
CLIN-005765  - 15 June 2025
CLIN-005766  - 15 July 2025 (Final Request)

CLIN-005767  These requests have now all exceeded the GDPR Article 12(3) response window. No lawful extensions were issued. No formal responses were received. No rectifications were made.

CLIN-005768  Accordingly, the institution is now in active and unresolved breach of GDPR Article 12(3), Article 16 and Article 5(1)(d).

CLIN-005769  2. Ongoing Article 15 Subject Access Request (Teaching Interaction)

CLIN-005770  Submitted: 7 July 2025
CLIN-005771  Statutory Deadline: 7 August 2025

CLIN-005772  This request concerns a bedside teaching encounter on 10–11 July 2017, during my inpatient stay on St. Anthony’s Ward. I raised concern — in front of four medical trainees — that my core symptom of disabling bilateral foot pain had been ignored. The supervising clinician dismissed this concern in a way that was educationally modeled and normalized.

CLIN-005773  The hospital remains obligated to respond in full by 7 August 2025 and provide access to all documentation, logbooks, and governance records related to the incident.

CLIN-005774  Documentation of Procedural Mismanagement

CLIN-005775  On 14 July 2025, I received an acknowledgment email from Ms. Nuala Madden stating that rectification “is a right under GDPR but not an absolute right.” This offered no identification of the request referenced, no citation of Article 12(3) timelines, and no confirmation of processing status.

CLIN-005776  In response, I issued a detailed clarification the same day titled “Clarification and Legal Inference – Institutional Confusion Regarding GDPR Rights.”

CLIN-005777  As of today — 30 July 2025 — no correction, update, or engagement has been received.

CLIN-005778  Final Position and Enforcement Notification

CLIN-005779  As of 22 July 2025, Galway University Hospital entered into formal and ongoing breach of GDPR Articles 5(1)(d), 12(3), and 16.

CLIN-005780  No further internal engagement will be pursued. I will now be submitting a full complaint under Article 77 GDPR to the Data Protection Commission, including all Article 16 requests and timelines, email correspondence and image evidence, a documented summary of institutional missteps, and this letter and attachments.

CLIN-005781  I continue to await your lawful response to the Article 15 access request due 7 August 2025. All other matters are closed.

CLIN-005782  Attachments included:
CLIN-005783  - Final Article 16 Rectification Request (15 July 2025)
CLIN-005784  - Clarification and Legal Inference – GDPR Misinterpretation (14 July 2025)
CLIN-005785  - Acknowledgment Email – Nuala Madden (14 July 2025, screenshot)
CLIN-005786  - Article 15 Access Request – Teaching Incident (7 July 2025)

CLIN-005787  Sincerely,
CLIN-005788  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005796  To Whom It May Concern,

CLIN-005797  I acknowledge receipt of your e-mail to the Data Protection Commission (DPC).

CLIN-005798  In line with our Customer Service Charter, we aim to reply to the concerns raised by you within 20 working days, though complex complaints may require further time for initial assessment. In doing so, we will communicate clearly, providing you with relevant information or an update regarding your correspondence.

CLIN-005799  What can I do to progress my concern?

CLIN-005800  In the meantime, if your concern relates to processing of your personal data by an organisation (a “data controller”), or you wish to exercise your data protection rights (for example, access, erasure, rectification), you may wish to contact the data protection officer for that organisation in writing in the first instance. You may wish to forward copies of all written exchanges with the data controller to the DPC if you remain dissatisfied with the response you receive from them. You should send this documentation to info@dataprotection.ie and include the above reference number.

CLIN-005801  What if I have already contacted an organisation (“data controller”) about my concerns?

CLIN-005802  If you have already exchanged written correspondence with the data controller, and have not included this information with your initial contact with the DPC, you should send this documentation to info@dataprotection.ie quoting the case reference shown above.

CLIN-005803  Please ensure that any copies of correspondence sent/received by email include the following: To, From, Sent date and time.

CLIN-005804  What happens when I send the DPC additional correspondence or documents?

CLIN-005817  From: Liam Moloney
CLIN-005818  Date: 10-08-2025
CLIN-005819  Re: Supplementary Evidence – Case C-25-7-2074

CLIN-005820  Dear Mr Clinton,

CLIN-005821  I am writing to provide supplementary evidence for my ongoing complaint under reference C-25-7-2074.

CLIN-005822  The purpose of this submission is to give the Commission a concentrated set of original, contemporaneous documents which together show:

CLIN-005823  - How the inaccuracy in my personal data was created in 2017.
CLIN-005824  - How it has been actively preserved and defended into 2025.
CLIN-005825  - How it continues to operate as a live breach, including in a teaching environment where it risks propagation to future clinical and governance practice.

CLIN-005826  Enclosed documents:

CLIN-005827  - GP Referral Letter – 29 June 2017 — records disabling bilateral foot pain, functional decline, and risk to employment; frames the case as a differential diagnosis, with foot pain front-and-centre.
CLIN-005828  - Hospital Discharge Summary – 11 July 2017 — the patient states it omits foot pain or associated pathology, records a Parkinson’s diagnosis made outside declared specialist competence, and shows no levodopa challenge or relevant investigation.
CLIN-005829  - Dr Monaghan’s Rebuttal – 25 March 2025 — confirms Parkinson’s diagnosis, acknowledges levodopa responsiveness of pain but frames it as incidental; the patient states it offers no explanation for omission of presenting symptom or failure to refer after declaring lack of expertise.

CLIN-005830  Taken together, these three documents show, in my submission, that the inaccuracy in my record was not accidental, but the product of an early substitution of facts which has since been entrenched. The GP’s urgent and specific description was replaced with a narrower, incorrect framing, and this altered version has been preserved through eight years, despite pharmacological evidence to the contrary and three formal Article 16 rectification requests.

CLIN-005831  Why urgency is required:

CLIN-005832  - This is a live breach of Articles 5(1)(d) and 16 GDPR.
CLIN-005833  - The inaccurate record remains in active use in a hospital setting.
CLIN-005834  - The breach is also present in a teaching hospital environment, exposing trainees to a culture where inaccuracy is tolerated and defended.
CLIN-005835  - I have already waited eight years for this to be addressed; the hospital has already ignored three lawful requests for rectification.
CLIN-005836  - Given the clinical and cultural risk, I respectfully submit there is no public interest in further delay.

CLIN-005837  I trust the Commission will give this supplementary evidence due consideration in the current case, and I urge early action in light of the ongoing and systemic nature of the breach.

CLIN-005838  Yours sincerely,
CLIN-005839  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005860  **Date / period:** 10 September 2025
CLIN-005861  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient submissions to Data Protection Commission
CLIN-005862  **Provenance:** Connected Gmail messages 199335fc56930ca6 and 19933d598335eaf0
CLIN-005863  **Forensic status:** Primary regulator-facing evidence of the patient’s theory after receipt of the two 6 September Article 16 refusals and after the SIMT outcome.
CLIN-005864  **Source-status note:** The source preserves several patient legal/clinical conclusions and some date formulations that differ from other canonical sources (for example, references to a 1 September SIMT outcome). Those propositions are preserved as communicated and are not silently corrected. The canonical SIMT source elsewhere is dated 3 September 2025.

CLIN-005865  ## First submission — executive addenda

CLIN-005866  Dear Case Officer,

CLIN-005867  I write to provide a brief executive summary and to submit supplementary evidence in relation to Case C-25-7-2074 (Liam Moloney v Galway University Hospitals).

CLIN-005868  Since filing my formal complaint on 7 August 2025, two new developments have occurred:

CLIN-005869  - Entrenched Refusal of Rights – On 10 September 2025, I received two refusal letters from Galway University Hospital (dated 6 September). These letters arrived well after the statutory deadlines and after my complaint was already before the Commission. They assert that (a) misdiagnosis is “an opinion at the time” and therefore not inaccurate data, and (b) QPS closure letters are not personal data. If accepted, these positions would nullify rectification rights entirely for patients in healthcare.

CLIN-005870  - Fresh Breach of Article 18 GDPR – On 4 August 2025 I invoked Article 18 to restrict processing of disputed data. Despite this, GUH convened a Serious Incident Management Team review on 27 August and issued an outcome letter on 1 September relying on the same disputed records. This constitutes unlawful processing under restriction and demonstrates systemic disregard for GDPR obligations.

CLIN-005871  These two developments confirm that GUH is not simply failing to respond but is actively adopting positions that deny patients’ rights under GDPR. The refusal letters and Article 18 breach shift this case from one of delay into one of systemic evasion of accountability.

CLIN-005872  For your convenience, I have enclosed a supplementary document containing two structured addenda (with Exhibits A–D) which set out these matters in detail.

CLIN-005873  Thank you for adding this material to my file and for your ongoing consideration.

CLIN-005874  Yours sincerely,
CLIN-005875  Liam Moloney


CLIN-005876  ## Second submission — further evidence

CLIN-005877  Subject: Case C-25-7-2074 – Further Evidence: Refusal of Refusals, Ongoing Inaccuracy, and Contradictions

CLIN-005878  Dear Ms Kenworthy,

CLIN-005879  I am submitting further evidence for case C-25-7-2074 regarding Galway University Hospital’s handling of my rectification requests.

CLIN-005880  On 10 September 2025, I received two refusal letters from GUH, both dated 6 September 2025. These letters are procedurally invalid for the following reasons:

CLIN-005881  -

CLIN-005882  My rectification requests of 12 June 2025 and 15 June 2025 fell due by 12 July and 15 July 2025 under Article 12(3).

CLIN-005883  -

CLIN-005884  No acknowledgment was issued, no reply provided, and no extension invoked.

CLIN-005885  -

CLIN-005886  On 30 July 2025, I formally closed the case statutorily on grounds of non-compliance.

CLIN-005887  -

CLIN-005888  On 4 August 2025, I placed GUH on litigation hold and invoked Article 18 restriction.

CLIN-005889  -

CLIN-005890  On 7 August 2025, I filed this complaint with the DPC.

CLIN-005891  -

CLIN-005892  Despite all of this, GUH issued refusal letters on 6 September 2025 — long after closure, long after restriction, and long after escalation to the DPC.

CLIN-005893  The apology “for the delay” in those letters cannot be accepted. The issue is not delay; the issue is breach of law.

CLIN-005894  Failure of Proportionate Remedy

CLIN-005895  Both letters refuse not only rectification but also annotation, even though annotation is the proportionate solution under Article 16 when disputed opinions cannot be erased. Instead of resolving contradictions, GUH declined both rectification and annotation, and simply told me that if I disagreed, my “next port of call” was the Data Protection Commission. This demonstrates deflection, not compliance.

CLIN-005896  Contradictions in the Record

CLIN-005897  My file continues to contain contradictions that leave it inaccurate under Article 5(1)(d). Examples include:

CLIN-005898  -

CLIN-005899  Apology vs. No Deficit

CLIN-005900  -

CLIN-005901  Dr Monaghan letter, 25 March 2025: “I am truly very sorry indeed… I have realised the pain and discomfort he has gone through.”

CLIN-005902  -

CLIN-005903  SIMT outcome letter, 1 September 2025: “The SIMT determined there was no deficit in the care provided.”

CLIN-005904  -

CLIN-005905  Both cannot be true: either harm occurred or it did not.

CLIN-005906  -

CLIN-005907  Regret vs. Caution

CLIN-005908  -

CLIN-005909  2017 correspondence quoted in Dr Monaghan letter, 25 March 2025: “Indeed in time to come – we may regret that we did not start sooner.”

CLIN-005910  -

CLIN-005911  Same letter, 25 March 2025: “My reluctance… was because of concerns of dyskinesia… especially in a younger person.”

CLIN-005912  -

CLIN-005913  Harmful delay is both admitted and justified simultaneously.

CLIN-005914  -

CLIN-005915  Benefit Acknowledged vs. Diagnostic Value Denied

CLIN-005916  -

CLIN-005917  Dr Monaghan letter, 25 March 2025: “He knows very well that once the Sinemet hits his system that the feet feel better…”

CLIN-005918  -

CLIN-005919  Same letter: “Sinemet should not do anything for pain…”

CLIN-005920  -

CLIN-005921  The effect is both acknowledged and denied, leaving the file incoherent.

CLIN-005922  -

CLIN-005923  Timeline Rewrite

CLIN-005924  -

CLIN-005925  Clinic letter, 10 March 2025: “First of all, we will start Sinemet controlled release for Liam.”

CLIN-005926  -

CLIN-005927  SIMT outcome letter, 1 September 2025: “Sinemet was first prescribed in 2021.”

CLIN-005928  -

CLIN-005929  These are irreconcilable: CR was initiated in 2025, not 2021.

CLIN-005930  -

CLIN-005931  Opinion vs. Data

CLIN-005932  -

CLIN-005933  Refusal letter, 6 September 2025: “Misdiagnosis constitutes a ‘professional opinion at the time’ and therefore cannot be considered inaccurate data.”

CLIN-005934  -

CLIN-005935  Yet these same “opinions” are relied upon in internal reviews and stored in my record. Under GDPR, opinions relating to an identifiable person are personal data and subject to accuracy obligations.

CLIN-005936  -

CLIN-005937  Restriction Ignored

CLIN-005938  -

CLIN-005939  My Article 18 restriction, 4 August 2025: disputed diagnosis restricted.

CLIN-005940  -

CLIN-005941  SIMT outcome, 1 September 2025: relied on those disputed entries.

CLIN-005942  -

CLIN-005943  Processing despite restriction is a clear violation of Article 18.

CLIN-005944  Not Historical – An Ongoing Health Crisis

CLIN-005945  This is not about historical paperwork. These contradictions sit in my active medical file, which continues to shape my treatment and safety. The hospital is standing over a file written by a clinician who openly disclaimed expertise in 2017. Every day the file remains uncorrected, I carry forward a record that is both inaccurate and unsafe.

CLIN-005946  Personal Plea

CLIN-005947  I need to stress that this is not only about contradictions in documents — it is about the collapse of my care. The entire file the hospital is standing over was written by a clinician who, in his discharge summary, openly stated: “I am not a movement-disorders specialist.” On the very first and second days of my admission in 2017, he made two clinical decisions that shaped everything that followed, and both devastated the possibility of me receiving appropriate care. Since then, every note in my file has been written by someone who had already disclaimed the relevant expertise.

CLIN-005948  As a result, I have not had an accurate diagnosis since 2017. I do not have a diagnosis that any specialist would stand over 100%. Even now, when I finally obtained complete relief from my pain through Sinemet CR, I only gained relief from that suffering — I did not gain the security of a reliable, accurate diagnosis. The hospital still has the duty it had in 2017, but instead of discharging it, it is entrenching its position and defending contradictions. This GDPR file now dominates my reality, making it very difficult to move forward while I live with the consequences of eight years of untreated pain.

CLIN-005949  I do understand that this is a particularly difficult situation for the hospital. But the hospital is not a person. The hospital is not a person in pain. The hospital is a data controller; it is an institution, a branch of society designed to provide care. And yet in this case, it is doing everything in its power to avoid facing the reality that I still need exactly the same help I needed when I first presented in 2017. The hospital has gone from being the people who could help me, to being the people who are actively hindering that help.

CLIN-005950  Request

CLIN-005951  I ask that the DPC note the following:

CLIN-005952  -

CLIN-005953  The refusals and apology of 6 September 2025 are procedurally void, issued after statutory closure, after litigation hold, and after escalation.

CLIN-005954  -

CLIN-005955  The contradictions listed above remain unresolved and breach Article 5(1)(d).

CLIN-005956  -

CLIN-005957  GUH has failed to act proportionately by refusing annotation and deflecting responsibility to the DPC.

CLIN-005958  -

CLIN-005959  This is a live health crisis, not a historic one, and accuracy of the file is a present duty.

CLIN-005960  I respectfully request that this evidence be added to case C-25-7-2074 and that the DPC require GUH to comply with their obligations under GDPR Articles 5(1)(d), 16, and 18.

CLIN-005961  “I am not simply contesting paperwork; I am asking for the accuracy of my medical file so that I can have a safe foundation for ongoing care. This is not about the past — it is about whether I can receive care today.”

CLIN-005962  Yours sincerely,

CLIN-005963  Liam Moloney

CLIN-005966  # DOCUMENT 77 — QPS Governance Re-engagement — Present Reliance on the 2025 Configuration

CLIN-005967  **Date / period:** 15 January 2026
CLIN-005968  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient correspondence to GUH QPS

The primary December specialist letter has not been located in the reviewed corpus and remains a recorded evidence gap.

CLIN-005972  Dear Quality & Patient Safety Team,

CLIN-005973  I am writing to re-engage with Quality & Patient Safety in a governance capacity, arising from the procedural decisions taken in April 2025 in relation to my formal complaint of 19 March 2025, and the consequences of those decisions as they now stand in January 2026.

CLIN-005974  This correspondence does not seek to reopen a complaint, revisit clinical judgment, or challenge the appropriateness of the handling of my complaint at the time. It seeks to examine whether the procedural configuration adopted by QPS in April 2025 has resulted in a governance position that now prevents the institution from lawfully reconciling data it knows to be incomplete, and which continues to be relied upon.

CLIN-005975  1. Context and Procedural History (Brief)

CLIN-005976  My complaint of 19 March 2025 was assessed by QPS and, by letter dated 15 April 2025, was classified as relating to matters of clinical judgment and processed under the Health Act complaints pathway. That classification decision routed the matter back to the treating clinician and was treated as resolved upon receipt of a clinician response.

CLIN-005977  Subsequently, the matter was folded into the Incident Management Framework, culminating in SIMT review and an outcome stating that there was “no deficit in care.”

CLIN-005978  I acknowledge that these decisions were taken within established frameworks and without the benefit of later developments. I am not challenging their propriety at the time they were made.

CLIN-005979  2. The Present Governance Issue (as of January 2026)

CLIN-005980  The issue now arising is not retrospective dissatisfaction. It is a present-tense governance problem.

CLIN-005981  As of today, I state that there is an authoritative diagnosis of dystonia made in December 2025, explicitly not Parkinson’s disease; that this diagnosis explains the original 2017 referral and presenting features; that it materially undermines the completeness and accuracy of the data set upon which administrative finality was achieved; and that the original diagnosis nevertheless remains operative and relied upon.

CLIN-005982  3. Why This Is a QPS Governance Question

CLIN-005983  This correspondence is directed to QPS because the procedural pathway chosen in April 2025 is the point at which the matter was definitively classified, routed, and closed for institutional purposes.

CLIN-005984  I contend that this classification had downstream effects: contradiction could be absorbed without effect; governance processes could proceed on inherited assumptions; and no internal pathway existed to reconcile later material without contradicting earlier procedural acts.

CLIN-005985  4. The Question Requiring Governance Consideration

CLIN-005986  Can a public hospital lawfully continue to rely on, process, and disseminate a diagnosis that it now knows was administratively finalised before scientific sequence was complete, where correction is resisted solely because revisiting it would destabilise downstream institutional decisions?

CLIN-005987  This question does not ask QPS to adjudicate diagnosis, assign fault, or reopen a complaint. It asks whether the current configuration remains lawful and safe to maintain, given what the institution now knows.

CLIN-005988  5. Purpose of This Engagement

CLIN-005989  I am not seeking adversarial escalation, redress, or attribution of blame. I am seeking clarity as to whether the procedural architecture adopted in April 2025 still permits lawful action in January 2026.

CLIN-005990  If QPS considers that this issue properly lies with another governance function, I ask for confirmation of that position. If QPS considers that continued reliance on the current data state raises governance risk, I ask for confirmation that the issue has been escalated appropriately.

CLIN-005991  Yours sincerely,
CLIN-005992  Liam Moloney
CLIN-005993  Patient / Data Subject

CLIN-005994  ---

CLIN-005995  # DOCUMENT 78 — National HSE Quality & Patient Safety Notification — Administrative Foreclosure of Diagnostic Sequence

CLIN-005996  **Date / period:** 15 January 2026
CLIN-005997  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient submission to HSE National Quality & Patient Safety / CEO Office

Statements within it such as “facts are not contested,” “administrative foreclosure,” and claims about safeguards are the author’s governance propositions, not HSE findings. Inclusion establishes notice and the evolution of the patient’s framing.

CLIN-006001  ## Transmission email

CLIN-006002  Dear National Director of Quality & Patient Safety,

CLIN-006003  I am writing to place before the HSE Executive an enclosed Governance & Patient Safety Notification concerning a documented failure mode within the public health system, namely the administrative and statutory finalisation of a provisional diagnostic hypothesis before completion of diagnostic sequence.

CLIN-006004  This submission is not a complaint seeking individual sanction, nor does it ask the HSE to adjudicate clinical diagnosis or professional conduct. It is offered in the public interest as a sentinel governance notification, identifying a structural risk to patient safety and system correctability.

CLIN-006005  For completeness, I note that in April and May 2025, senior operational leadership within the HSE structure — including Saolta Group executive management and HSE West Risk Management — were formally notified of the underlying issue now reframed in the enclosed document. Those notifications were not substantively engaged with at the time.

CLIN-006006  This present submission is therefore made not as a first-instance escalation, but as a national-level governance re-engagement, in recognition that the issue identified is systemic, repeatable, and extends beyond local hospital processes.

CLIN-006007  The enclosed notification confines itself deliberately to matters of governance, escalation safeguards, statutory finality, and complaints architecture. It does not rely on hindsight, later diagnostic developments, or assertions of fault. Its sole purpose is to support institutional learning and risk prevention.

CLIN-006008  I submit this material respectfully and in good faith, and I trust it will be received in the spirit of patient safety and system integrity in which it is offered.

CLIN-006009  Yours faithfully,
CLIN-006010  Liam Moloney


CLIN-006011  ## Enclosed five-page Governance & Patient Safety Notification — text layer

CLIN-006012  Governance & Patient Safety Notification
CLIN-006013  to the HSE Executive
CLIN-006014  Administrative Foreclosure of Diagnostic Sequence and
CLIN-006015  Failure of Safeguards


CLIN-006016  1. Purpose of this Notification
CLIN-006017  This document is submitted as a governance and patient‑safety notification to the Health
CLIN-006018  Service Executive (HSE) Executive and National Quality & Patient Safety functions.

CLIN-006019  It is not a complaint seeking individual sanction, clinical adjudication, or retrospective diagnosis.

CLIN-006020  Its sole purpose is to place before the HSE Executive a documented failure mode within the
CLIN-006021  public health system whereby:

CLIN-006022          A provisional diagnostic hypothesis was administratively and statutorily finalised
CLIN-006023          before completion of diagnostic sequence, resulting in structural foreclosure of later
CLIN-006024          correction and prolonged downstream harm.

CLIN-006025  The case functions as a sentinel governance event and raises questions about the adequacy
CLIN-006026  of national safeguards, escalation frameworks, and complaint‑handling architecture.




CLIN-006027  2. Scope and Jurisdiction
CLIN-006028  This notification engages the HSE’s responsibilities in respect of:

CLIN-006029     ●​    National clinical governance and escalation safeguards
CLIN-006030     ●​    Patient safety learning and systemic risk identification
CLIN-006031     ●​    Oversight of statutory certification pathways (including LTI)
CLIN-006032     ●​    Integrity of complaints handling and escalation frameworks
CLIN-006033     ●​    Interaction between clinical decision‑making and administrative finality

CLIN-006034  The HSE is not asked to determine diagnostic correctness, clinical negligence, or professional
CLIN-006035  misconduct. Those matters fall elsewhere.
CLIN-006036  The HSE is asked to examine whether its systems permit — or fail to prevent — premature
CLIN-006037  administrative closure of unresolved diagnostic processes.




CLIN-006038  3. Factual Basis (High‑Level, Non‑Disputable)
CLIN-006039  The following facts are drawn exclusively from contemporaneous institutional records:

CLIN-006040     1.​ A GP referral (June 2017) identified a foot‑centred gait failure as the initiating clinical
CLIN-006041         problem and requested neurological assessment. No diagnosis was asserted.
CLIN-006042     2.​ During inpatient assessment, diagnostic uncertainty and atypical features were explicitly
CLIN-006043         acknowledged.
CLIN-006044     3.​ A principal diagnostic discriminator (dopaminergic therapy) was identified
CLIN-006045         contemporaneously and deliberately deferred.
CLIN-006046     4.​ The case was escalated within the department, invoking senior authority and
CLIN-006047         constructive knowledge.
CLIN-006048     5.​ Despite unresolved diagnostic sequence, statutory Long Term Illness certification for
CLIN-006049         Parkinson’s disease was executed prior to discharge.
CLIN-006050     6.​ The discharge summary displaced the unresolved initiating problem rather than resolving
CLIN-006051         or falsifying it.
CLIN-006052     7.​ Once statutory finality attached, the diagnostic frame was never substantively reopened
CLIN-006053         despite repeated encounters with the unresolved primary signal.
CLIN-006054     8.​ Subsequent complaint handling misclassified the matter as one of “clinical judgment,”
CLIN-006055         blocking escalation and independent review.

CLIN-006056  These facts are not contested and do not depend on hindsight or later diagnostic developments.




CLIN-006057  4. The Governance Failure Identified
CLIN-006058  4.1 Administrative Foreclosure

CLIN-006059  The core failure is not diagnostic error.

CLIN-006060  It is the conferral of administrative and statutory finality on a provisional diagnostic
CLIN-006061  hypothesis before completion of diagnostic sequence.

CLIN-006062  Once finality attached:

CLIN-006063     ●​ downstream systems were entitled to rely on the diagnosis;
CLIN-006064     ●​ correction became institutionally unsafe;
CLIN-006065     ●​ uncertainty was stabilised rather than resolved;
CLIN-006066     ●​ and subsequent governance mechanisms operated within a closed frame.

CLIN-006067  This is a systemic effect, not an individual lapse.




CLIN-006068  4.2 Failure of Safeguards

CLIN-006069  The case exposes the absence or failure of safeguards that should prevent:

CLIN-006070     ●​   statutory certification prior to diagnostic completion;
CLIN-006071     ●​   escalation decisions that defer principal discriminators without parallel protections;
CLIN-006072     ●​   displacement of unresolved initiating problems at the point of discharge;
CLIN-006073     ●​   complaints misclassification where governance and record‑integrity issues are raised.

CLIN-006074  At no stage did the system require reconciliation between:

CLIN-006075     ●​ acknowledged uncertainty,
CLIN-006076     ●​ deferred discrimination,
CLIN-006077     ●​ and the execution of administrative finality.




CLIN-006078  5. Complaints Handling as a Secondary Governance
CLIN-006079  Failure
CLIN-006080  When the issue was raised through formal complaints channels:

CLIN-006081     ●​ it was classified as a matter of “clinical judgment”;
CLIN-006082     ●​ escalation mechanisms were not applied despite clear procedural requests;
CLIN-006083     ●​ the same office that closed the complaint re‑engaged with it;
CLIN-006084     ●​ alternative internal frameworks were substituted without addressing the procedural
CLIN-006085        defects;
CLIN-006086     ●​ and silence or non‑response functioned as de facto closure.

CLIN-006087  This prevented the governance failure from being examined at the appropriate level and allowed
CLIN-006088  reliance on the foreclosed record to continue.




CLIN-006089  6. Why This Is a National Issue
CLIN-006090  This case is not idiosyncratic.
CLIN-006091  The failure mode identified is structural and repeatable:

CLIN-006092      1.​ Provisional diagnosis under uncertainty
CLIN-006093      2.​ Identification but deferral of a principal discriminator
CLIN-006094      3.​ Escalation to senior authority
CLIN-006095      4.​ Execution of statutory or administrative finality
CLIN-006096      5.​ Record hardening
CLIN-006097      6.​ Complaints deflection and avoidance of escalation

CLIN-006098  Any system permitting this sequence creates risk for future patients, regardless of ultimate
CLIN-006099  diagnostic outcome.




CLIN-006100  7. What This Notification Seeks
CLIN-006101  The author respectfully requests that the HSE Executive:

CLIN-006102      1.​ Recognise administrative foreclosure of diagnostic sequence as a patient‑safety
CLIN-006103          risk.
CLIN-006104      2.​ Review whether current governance frameworks adequately prevent statutory finality
CLIN-006105          attaching to unresolved diagnostic processes.
CLIN-006106      3.​ Examine the interaction between escalation, senior advice, and responsibility for
CLIN-006107          diagnostic sequencing.
CLIN-006108      4.​ Review complaints classification practices where governance and record‑integrity issues
CLIN-006109          are raised.
CLIN-006110      5.​ Consider whether national guidance or safeguards are required to prevent recurrence.

CLIN-006111  No individual sanction is sought.

CLIN-006112  No retrospective clinical determination is requested.




CLIN-006113  8. Status of This Document
CLIN-006114  This document is a governance notification, not a complaint.

CLIN-006115  It is submitted in good faith, in the public interest, and for the purpose of institutional learning
CLIN-006116  and patient safety improvement.

CLIN-006117  The underlying canonical case has not been submitted elsewhere in full and is retained as the
CLIN-006118  authoritative internal record from which this derivative is drawn. The governance issues
CLIN-006119  identified remain operative and unresolved as of January 2026, with ongoing reliance on the
CLIN-006120  administratively finalised record.
CLIN-006121  9. Closing Position
CLIN-006122  The central issue for the HSE is not whether a diagnosis was right or wrong.

CLIN-006123  It is whether the system allowed itself to become unable to correct itself.

CLIN-006124  Where administrative finality overtakes diagnostic sequence, patient safety depends not on
CLIN-006125  clinical excellence but on institutional courage.

CLIN-006126  This notification is offered to support that examination.

CLIN-006128  # DOCUMENT 79 — Article 18 Supplement to DPC — SIMT and Continued Reliance

CLIN-006129  **Date / period:** 27 January 2026
CLIN-006130  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient submission to Data Protection Commission
CLIN-006131  **Provenance:** Connected Gmail message 19bfff40b47101dd; substantive body preserved in 2026-01-27_DPC-Article18-Supplement.txt
CLIN-006132  **Forensic status:** Primary regulator-facing evidence that the patient presented the 4 August restriction notice, 3 September SIMT outcome and 26 January QPS response as one narrow Article 18 sequence.
CLIN-006133  **Source-status note:** Whether Article 18 legally prohibited the particular SIMT processing, and whether any exception applied, are matters for legal/regulatory determination. The source proves the issue placed before the DPC.

CLIN-006134  Purpose: To provide additional material confirming a completed and continuing Article 18 GDPR issue

CLIN-006135  Dear Case Officer,

CLIN-006136  Summary:
CLIN-006137  This submission supplements the existing assessment file with a narrow, document based issue under Article 18 GDPR.

CLIN-006138  On 4 August 2025, the data subject invoked Article 18 to restrict processing of disputed diagnostic data. Notwithstanding that restriction, the Serious Incident Management Team (SIMT) proceeded to rely on the restricted data to generate a definitive institutional outcome on 3 September 2025.

CLIN-006139  On 26 January 2026, the Quality & Patient Safety Department (QPS), on behalf of the controller, confirmed that SIMT relied on contemporaneous data and that the outcome remains valid and relied upon, with no escalation indicated.

CLIN-006140  Documents provided:
CLIN-006141  1) Article 18 restriction notice (4 August 2025) – copied to DPO and QPS.
CLIN-006142  2) SIMT outcome letter (3 September 2025).
CLIN-006143  3) QPS letter confirming continued reliance and non escalation (26 January 2026).

CLIN-006144  Issue for assessment

CLIN-006145  Article 18 restricts processing of disputed data to storage pending resolution. The controller has confirmed reliance on contemporaneous data that was under restriction at the time of reliance, and continues to stand over the resulting output. No qualification, notification, or reconciliation has been effected.

CLIN-006146  Questions for the controller

CLIN-006147  1) How does the controller reconcile reliance on data that was subject to an active Article 18 restriction at the time SIMT generated its outcome?
CLIN-006148  2) On what basis does the controller consider continued reliance on outputs generated from restricted data to be lawful under Articles 5(1)(d), 16, 18, 19, and 24 GDPR?

CLIN-006149  This submission is provided to assist assessment and does not seek clinical review or reopening of governance processes.

CLIN-006150  Warm regards,
CLIN-006151  Liam Moloney

CLIN-006168  # DOCUMENT 81 — QPS Transparency Note — Claimed Internal Inconsistency Between Clinical-Judgment Classification and Later Review

CLIN-006169  **Date / period:** 29 January 2026
CLIN-006170  **Voice / origin:** Primary patient correspondence to GUH QPS
CLIN-006171  **Provenance:** Connected Gmail message 19c0a951fcabda84; substantive body preserved in 2026-01-29_QPS-Transparency-Position.txt
CLIN-006172  **Forensic status:** Primary evidence of the patient’s mature January 2026 argument about the relationship between the April 2025 classification and the later PAR/SIMT process.
CLIN-006173  **Source-status note:** The conclusion that the original classification was “retroactively falsified,” and the wider propositions about entitlement to certainty and path dependence, are the patient’s analysis. The correspondence is evidence that this argument was communicated, not an institutional admission.

CLIN-006174  I am providing this for transparency, to clarify my position on where governance responsibility attaches. No response is required unless the institution wishes to engage at that level.

CLIN-006175  If I were to accept Quality & Patient Safety’s stated position of 26 January 2026 on its own terms, it still cannot stand. That position relies on an internally inconsistent chain of reasoning. The complaint was first classified as “solely clinical judgment,” thereby exempting it from examination. The institution then conducted substantive governance reviews (PAR and SIMT) that necessarily presupposed assessability and produced a finding of clinical appropriateness. That finding retroactively falsifies the original exemption.

CLIN-006176  Once SIMT reported, QPS was obliged, in my view, to revisit and reconcile its April 2025 classification. It did not do so. Instead, it continued to rely on both positions simultaneously. I contend that this leaves the governance record incoherent.

CLIN-006177  However, I said that dispute was secondary. Even if QPS had handled the complaint flawlessly in 2025–2026, the core failure would remain untouched. My position was that QPS inherited an already-closed diagnostic and administrative state fixed in 2017, and everything since was downstream behaviour inside that frame.

CLIN-006178  I concluded that engagement was only meaningful at the point where certainty was exercised without entitlement and governance responsibility first attached: 2017.

CLIN-006179  Liam Moloney
